Is it going negative and staying negative for consecutive months or quarters? I mean, how do you think about what you would need to see to kind of shift gears?

Lori Flees: Yes. Good question. So really, we look at retention. And if we start to see any significant changes out of the ordinary on customer retention, that's when I think we have to look at our pricing and whether or not that's a driver or whether it's other issues. Again, our retention has improved year-over-year. The promotional side or how you discount, and we've been doing a lot of work on offering the right discount at the right time to the right potential customer.

And obviously, with new customers, when you are facing customers that feel very constrained from a spend perspective, it's typically not the time that they're going to try something new unless you give them something compelling, a compelling reason to do it. And that's the area where we have to continue to adapt and figure out the right approach to new customer acquisition in the environment and in those markets that have more value -- higher value-conscious consumer base.

Michael Albanese: So just to kind of frame that, I mean, it sounds like the first lever you would pull would be from a marketing standpoint or maybe an incentive standpoint, but less so about price. Is that fair?

Lori Flees: Yes, that has been the historical operating approach for us, but also in the industry.

Michael Albanese: Okay. All right. And then just a follow-up, quick one on Breeze here. Obviously, you said it's running in line with your expectations, if not better. I think when you first bought the 160 stores, they were doing about $1.1 million in average AUVs. Can we kind of maybe quantify this a little bit? I'd be curious as to maybe some of the earlier stores that you integrated, how that $1.1 million has grown or is tracking relative to the nearly $2 million kind of overall system average.

John Willis: I'll take that one, Mike. For the 28 stores that we've converted, it's still early days, but we are seeing positive momentum, and they're performing at or slightly above where we would expect them to be based upon what we would normally do for stores that we acquire. Just as a reminder, we'll buy 30 to 40 Quick Lubes per year and convert those to the Valvoline brand and then they start climbing the curve. And generally, those stores do reach the AUV that we would expect and that we enjoy in our overall store base. And it's really a combination of things. Number one, there's a marketing playbook that we implement that helps drive that.

It brings in new customers and really drives the transaction growth in those stores. And number two, the Valvoline brand is very strong. It's well recognized. That tends to bring in traffic as well. And number three, we have a very long-standing approach to how we train our teams and how they execute in the store with our customers that's very, very consistent, which not only drives traffic on the front end, but also keeps those customers coming back and really drives the very high retention rate that we enjoy. All of those things in combination help these stores and should help the Breeze stores climb the curve.

They are very, very similar to many stores that we would acquire with the exception that, frankly, they are generally better operated than many of the stores we would acquire on a onesie or twosie basis throughout the course of the year. And so they already have a very strong base from which to grow. They just need the Valvoline playbook, training, systems and brand to actually make that come to fruition over the course of time as they mature.

Michael Albanese: That's great. Is it generally a linear progression?

John Willis: So generally, year 1, you'll see a little bit of a dip as we invest in the store.

Lori Flees: In profit. But in sales, it goes up.

John Willis: In profit. That's right. Sales will go up. Profit will go down a little bit because we're investing in the team, we're investing in marketing, et cetera. But then it becomes pretty linear from there in terms of the maturity curve after...

Lori Flees: It's about 3 years of linear and then you'll get improvements in year 4 and 5, but to a lesser extent. It's a typical ramp is what we're expecting for our new -- our store conversions.

Operator: Your next question is from the line of Kevin Condon with Baird.

Kevin Condon: A bit of a follow-up to the prior question. But in fiscal 2027, as some of those Breeze stores start entering the comp equation, is that a tailwind to same-store sales just as those small stores grow and benefit from the rebranding? Or I guess given your comments on the EBITDA drag being less in 2026 and some of those integration improvements hitting earlier, is the comp uplift from the maturation of that store maybe less than a typical curve?

John Willis: Yes. So as a reminder, we converted 28 stores in fiscal '26. We would expect those stores to enter the comp in our Q2. So typically, it's a year after acquisition that a store would enter the comp. And so that would imply the beginning of our Q2, the March quarter. And again, as a reminder, it's 28 stores on a base of 2,500-plus stores. So while we would expect it to be a tailwind, it will be very, very small at this point. But we do expect it -- those stores to contribute.

Lori Flees: Yes. It will be small and growing over the time we integrate. So as we integrate -- continue to integrate stores in '27 and they enter the comp, it will obviously build over time, but it will be fairly small at the start of the year.

Kevin Condon: Got you. So it's a year after you convert the stores to the Valvoline brand that they start contributing to that comp equation. So it's...

John Willis: No. The way to think about it is we acquired the Breeze stores on December 1 of last year. And so December 1 of this year will mark 1 year that we've owned them. And we converted 28 stores in fiscal -- during fiscal '26. And so those 28 stores will enter the comp basically at the beginning of our Q2, which is the March quarter. So come January, those stores will be part of the comp because we will have owned them for a year by then. And so that's the way to think about that.

Lori Flees: And our same-store sales comp guidance is based on Valvoline Instant Oil Change stores in the United States. It has excluded Canada, and it will exclude the Oil Changers branded stores. So it'll just be Valvoline branded stores in the U.S. and only stores that we've owned for 1 year or more. So consistent with the definition that we switched to.

Operator: Your next question is from the line of Scott Stember with ROTH Capital Partners.

Scott Stember: Trying to get a sense of some of the areas that you saw in certain markets weakness, trying to get a sense of a cadence if we've seen things get worse as the quarter progressed. Just trying to get a sense of how sensitive these customers in the -- I guess, in the lower income markets are as they really feel the full brunt of these increased prices for an oil change.

Lori Flees: Yes. I don't think it's so much around the price change of oil changes. I think these are customers that broadly are feeling constraints from the macro inflationary gas prices, et cetera. And in Q4, we also recognized that people are making a variety of choices, particularly around the holidays. And so we've also factored in the fact that in times where people are making trade-offs, they may defer until next quarter services that they are due if they're not finite. So we anticipate customers in lower income demographics to be making certain trade-offs in Q4. But the reality is they are not trading off maintenance. The behavior -- customers understand the importance of maintaining their vehicles.

And part of this is driven by the cost that it would be to upgrade their vehicle. So buying a new used vehicle, the cost of car loans, et cetera, is very prohibitive versus where many people are today or the cost of major repairs. So it's a timing issue for some of these customers, not necessarily not going to do it at all. And so I don't think we see this getting worse for an extended period of time. Obviously, if the economy, something that we're not forecasting, and the pressure becomes broader, we don't see our customers' behavior changing all that much. And our numbers based on small changes are not highly sensitive.

At a company of our size, that actually works in our favor.

Scott Stember: Got it. And then just last question, going back to Breeze integration and synergies. One of the synergies that we've talked about is they're adopting fleet services and the non-oil change NOCR. Can you just talk about how those trends are going, particularly as things are within the market have tightened up?

Lori Flees: Yes. Great question. We integrated our fleet teams probably about 4 months ago. We fully integrated them in this quarter. So our fleet team now is working on behalf of the entire network. And we have sort of in our national fleet accounts, we've included Breeze sites in all of the accounts that we could do so given the way that they bill and that those contracts are arranged. So we're seeing some good momentum, though we're in early days around the Breeze stores.

It's much easier once we convert them, but we have done quite a number of things to try to get some of that growth earlier, and that's contributed to some of the Breeze momentum that we saw in the year. And on NOCR -- sorry. I would just say on NOCR, they did a pretty good job. We've been working across services that Valvoline offers that they don't, services that they offered that we didn't on the Valvoline side. In some cases, we are picking up some offerings, as I mentioned, premium filters as an option for our guests. That's something that some of our franchise partners have already done and Oil Changers did.

So we're getting the -- we'll get the benefit of that across our system. And then on the Oil Changers side, we're trying to work through, and I think we've decided not to add services into Oil Changers until we convert them. And part of that is the systems lift that you have to make in order to add those things into your offering. So I think we're just trying to pace is the juice worth the squeeze or do we wait until we convert the store. And I think we've decided that adding things to the Oil Changers stores will be probably to a minimum until we convert the stores.

Operator: Your next question is from the line of David Lantz with Wells Fargo.

David Lantz: So you mentioned fleet penetration continues to grow and that revenues for that segment grew high teens in fiscal '26. So curious if you can size that contribution to the 7.9% comp for the year and what you're embedding for the '27 outlook?

John Willis: David, as you look at the fleet business, it's still -- it's growing very well. We're incredibly pleased with how that is progressing. But as a reminder, it still represents under 10% of total system-wide sales. Again, growing very quickly, but still relatively small, which gives us a lot of encouragement around the opportunity now and into the future as we continue to grow that business. But I think the way to think about it is it's growing faster than the system, but it's still less than 10% of the system. And so it will be a contributor, but it's still relatively small in terms of the percentage of contribution.

David Lantz: Got it. That's helpful. And then just two more quick ones. Can you talk about the cadence of openings throughout the year in 2027 as well as the cadence of comps through fiscal Q4 and anything that you're seeing on a quarter-to-date basis?

John Willis: So in terms of the cadence of comps, currently, we're enjoying obviously price that's ahead of where we were last year and will be for the first half of the year. So we would expect the comp to be at or even initially above the high end of the 5% to 7% outlook range that we've given for the first half of the year and then moderating as we start to lap the pricing that both we and our franchise partners took in Q3, primarily for us in Q4 more so for them, so that -- which would take us more to the, call it, the low end of that range probably for the second half of the year.

Your other question was around the cadence of store openings?

Lori Flees: So I think we have -- there's a seasonality to the way that new store openings happen. We typically, in Q4, because of the timing of summer and construction as well as the timing of M&A that's going to hit by the end of the year, we typically have a pretty good Q4, then it goes lighter, and we tend to be very back-end loaded. And we've tried hard to pull that forward and be less back-end loaded. But the reality is construction cycles and M&A don't always align up well with that.

So I think you should think about the cadence to be consistent if you look back over the last 3 years with kind of where we've been on average.

Operator: Your next question is from the line of John Babcock with Barclays.

John Babcock: I guess just first on product costs. I want to get a little clarity here. So I think you're targeting up 60% by the end of the quarter. If you could just confirm that's where you were? And then also, how much are costs up now relative to the same starting benchmark from, I think, probably the end of the last quarter?

John Willis: So in the quarter, we basically -- it came in as expected. Product -- finished lubricant costs were up about 60% and to be very clear, the pricing that we have taken has covered that cost increase. And so we feel very good about where we are there. I would say for the last few weeks, product cost has been pretty stable. We've seen some upward movement in the base oil index for Group III. But to be clear, the base oil index and the spot market are still very, very disconnected. And we haven't seen a lot of change from a spot market perspective. But yes, and we're happy about that. Less volatility is good.

Obviously, we're looking forward to seeing costs go the other way once the supply chain frees up over the course of time. But for now, product costs have been very stable for the last few weeks, and that's a good thing.

John Babcock: Okay. So it's fair to say that costs are basically up a little bit from the end of the quarter, but not up significantly.

John Willis: They're really not up versus the end of the quarter. It's been pretty stable for the last, call it, 4 to 6 weeks.

Lori Flees: Relative to the average for the quarter, they're slightly up, but where we exited...

Lori Flees: We're seeing a lot of stability. And we're not expecting any significant changes on the supply side, both to the positive or the negative. But we certainly will adapt if there are changes, and we're ready for that.

John Babcock: Okay. And then next question, I know like fuel prices don't always tend to fully recover back for whatever reason. I'm just wondering -- and I know you don't have a lot of precedence in terms of the market and like where it is today. So maybe this is a bad question. But just generally, do base oil costs tend to fully recover when crude oil costs come down? Or what sort of movement do you tend to see?

John Willis: I would say over the course of time, that will happen. But to be clear, the base oil market and the crude oil market don't necessarily run in tandem. For a long time, they did. But over the past couple of years, even well before what we're in right now, started to unfold, those 2 things had disconnected themselves. Again, eventually, and over the course of time, it will match up. But as you look at base oil, it's a supply-demand phenomenon really. And right now, we're still tight, especially in Group III.

And even as you see crude oil futures and gasoline prices, which do tend to track pretty quickly and pretty closely, as you've seen those things change, again, the base oil market really hasn't changed. And that's all about supply and demand, especially in Group III, which remains tight.

Lori Flees: And for now, it's not just about oil flow, it's also about refining capacity. And some of the refining capacity is shut down because of the lack of supply, and it will take time for that to get back up and running once supply lines are fully loaded. So I think that's also part of the disconnect that we're planning for. So we're taking the approach in FY '27 that the environment remains the same as it relates to supply and cost. And I think overall, we're ready to execute within that and deliver really good profitable growth.

John Babcock: All right. That's very helpful. One last one, if you don't mind me squeezing this in. Just obviously, you had like really good cost control during the quarter, and it sounds like you're continuing to target cost across the company. Do you have any specific cost reduction targets that you have for the year? Or maybe you prefer not to go down that route?

John Willis: Yes. We prefer to not go down that path. I mean, we're continuing to work internally with the team to find efficiencies in the system and to execute against those. But we don't have any targets that we've talked about publicly. It's really all about executing well, continuing to find those efficiencies and then execute on those.

Operator: Your final question is from the line of Steven Zaccone with Citi.

Steven Zaccone: Great. It's very confusing with the base oil cost environment. I imagine it's very difficult to manage this as well. But I guess a simple question here, right? If we think about base oil costs kind of staying the same, right, and being stable, at what point would the gross margin rate get back to a level you were before we had all this disruption?

John Willis: Yes. I think the way we're looking at it right now, as we've said in our outlook, we're expecting the total gross margin compression for the full year of 100 basis points. And the implication is, as we said, approximately 200 to 250 basis points of compression kind of in the first half of the year, and you could infer that Q1 would be at the high end of that. And over the course of the year, we expect that to moderate.

So I would say all things being equal, if they ever are, all things being equal, we would expect to close that gap probably towards the end of the fiscal year, most likely based on where our outlook is.

Steven Zaccone: Okay. That's very helpful. Final one I had was just on the national advertising campaign. So I guess you're having a little bit of an expansion this year in terms of the fund. So maybe just talk about would that result in a comp lift? Like how do you think through that?

Lori Flees: Yes. Great question. So on the company side, in '26, we talked about flipping some of our local brand spend to national advertising, and we did see efficiency benefits on that, particularly in the fourth quarter. In FY '27, our entire system will start our national advertising fund, which will -- when you look at our financials, it will show that advertising costs are up at a greater rate, but this is more franchise funds flowing through and us spending them. So it's going to look a little odd on the P&L, but it is really just the franchise contributing to an advertising fund that we manage on behalf of the system.

And that increase in spend is going to drive more impressions at a much lower cost, which then drives better efficiency in the lower funnel. And what does that mean lower funnel? We get more people coming to search for Valvoline, come to Valvoline owned sites to learn more about our product and come to our stores where we're not paying for that traffic. And so that's going to allow us to use the money more wisely to actually attract and acquire more new customers into the system.

And that's part of the reason why when Kevin talked about total growth in transactions, we expect total growth in transactions to be the same or higher in this fiscal year than '26. And some of that is driven by what we believe will happen in the national ad fund. Now obviously, we will look to overperform, but we're early on in this. And so as we learn, we'll obviously update our expectations.

Operator: We have reached the end of the Q&A. This concludes today's call. Thank you for attending. You may now disconnect.

Hometown of accused US military plane saboteur is a known magnet for anti-Israel activism

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two people were arrested by Irish police after agitators gathered at Trinity College Dublin while former President Joe Biden made a surprise visit on Tuesday (Credit: Credit: PA Media via Reuters).

The European hometown of the man who single-handedly destroyed a U.S. military transport aircraft in an act of apparent protest has a rich history of pro-Palestinian support.

This includes the town council for Cork, Ireland, barring Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu from entering its city limits, citing his "illegal occupation of Palestine and bordering countries" and the continuous pro-Palestinian protests taking place every Saturday since October 2023, organized by the Cork Palestine Solidarity Campaign (CPSC).

Cork’s opposition to Israel adds context to the possible motives for why Daithí O’Corráin, an Irish national, allegedly destroyed a U.S. military C-130 Hercules aircraft in April. The incident also draws attention to several anti-Israel groups that have sprung up in Ireland in recent years.

IRELAND’S ELITES HAVE FOUND THEIR NEW RELIGION: HATING ISRAEL

Pro-Palestinian activists from the Ireland Palestine Solidarity Campaign, supported by members of left-wing parties including People Before Profit and the Socialist Party and students, participate in the National March for Palestine from the Garden of Remembrance to O'Connell Street and Leinster House, on May 18, 2024, in Dublin, Ireland. (Artur Widak/NurPhoto via Getty Images)

Ireland’s anti-Israel foothold became international news in April when O’Corráin, a Cork native, barricaded himself inside a C-130, a U.S. military transport plane. Once inside, he tore out critical wiring, destroyed gauges, contaminated fuel tanks and even poured corrosive chemicals into the aircraft, according to the U.S. Department of Justice.

Although his motives remain unclear, several groups from Cork interpreted his actions as a form of resistance against Israel and U.S. military interventionism.

In one such case, a Cork business owner named Tracey Ryan launched an initiative to raise €20,000 for O’Corráin's legal defense, praising his efforts to resist "the unwanted presence of U.S. military in Ireland in the face of their illegal war & support of Israel’s genocide."

"We, Daithí's friends and comrades, are raising funds to support him through his court case. We would really appreciate your support & will keep you updated here on ways to support Daithí," Ryan wrote in the description of the fundraising page.

According to her personal Facebook profile, Ryan joined a bus of pro-Palestinian activists who went to support O’Corráin at his court appearance. "Bus going to the high court to support Daithí on Thursday!!" Ryan wrote.

O’Corráin has also received support from CPSC, the Cork pro-Palestinian group.

As news of the incident spread, CPSC co-chair Anne McShane posted on her Palestinian advocacy Facebook page in support of O’Corráin, encouraging viewers to send him letters of encouragement after his incarceration.

"Daithi needs our support in order to keep up his morale. Letters are a lifeline for prisoners," an April 17 post read.

CPSC spearheads anti-Israel demonstrations in Cork. In addition to hosting several pro-Palestinian fundraising initiatives, the group facilitates weekly Saturday demonstrations and invites speakers. At one point, CPSC’s website featured a fundraising site for O’Corráin, but the page has since been removed.

INSIDE THE PROTEST PLAYBOOK TO VILIFY ISRAEL ON 'GLOBAL DAY OF ACTION'

Left: Smashed cockpit instruments inside a U.S. military C-130H Hercules aircraft allegedly destroyed at Shannon Airport in Ireland on April 11, 2026. Right: A separate U.S. Air Force C-130 Hercules aircraft at RAF Fairford in Gloucestershire, England, on March 18, 2026. (U.S. Department of Justice; Leon Neal/Getty Images)

In CPSC’s own words, the group is part of a larger pro-Palestinian movement.

"It’s not just the city. Towns across all of County Cork stand in solidarity with Palestinians, including Ballincollig, Bandon, Cobh, Charleville, Clonakilty, Fermoy, Kinsale, Macroom, Mallow, Mitchelstown, Skibbereen and more," the group states on its website.

"Cork Palestine Solidarity Campaign is a branch of the Irish Palestine Solidarity Campaign (IPSC)," it added.

As part of its sister groups’ efforts "to foster links between Palestine and Irish institutions and organizations," CPSC and IPSC have both applauded Cork’s city council efforts to promote pro-Palestinian policies.

"Well done to the Cork City Council which passed a motion … calling for the Central Bank to end the sale of Israeli bonds," one Facebook post reads.

The groups have also held Cork up as a template for Ireland’s national policy.

"We call on the Irish government to follow the example of both Dublin and Cork city councils by officially recognizing Israeli Apartheid – and acting to end any and all links to this regime," another statement reads.

For its own part, the Cork City Council has earned the support of IPSC and CPSC through a range of its official resolutions, making the town’s formal position to condemn any of Ireland’s ties to Israel.

In addition to banning Netanyahu from entering the city's limits in a 41-8 vote in 2024, Cork’s council has flown the Palestinian flag over city hall on U.N. International Day of Solidarity and called on Ireland to ban trade from Israeli settlements.

Cork has even passed a "twinning" mechanism with the Palestinian city of Nablus — a largely symbolic partnership.

IRISH PM REJECTS ANTISEMITISM CONCERNS IN IRELAND AMID DRAMATIC VOTE OVER BOYCOTTING ISRAEL GAME

Conoe Kelliher from Ballyvourney holds a Palestinian flag whilst attending a watch-along party in the town square of Ireland's Eurovision entrant Bambie Thug's hometown of Macroom in Co Cork, as the Eurovision final takes place at Malmo Arena on Saturday, May 11, 2024. (Noel Sweeney/PA Images via Getty Images)

Fergal Dennehy, the lord mayor of Cork from 2025 to 2026, hosted the mayor of Nablus in February, calling the partnership "just the beginning of what we hope will be a wonderful relationship between our two great cities."

Neither CPSC nor Cork City Council responded to Fox News Digital's requests for comment on the city's pro-Palestinian positions or about connections to O’Corráin.

O’Corráin is incarcerated in Ireland and faces criminal charges in the U.S. for "destruction of government property," valued at $60 million.

Huge New Nvidia Bug Is Ruining Gaming Hardware, Users Say

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia has not been having a good couple of weeks

Nvidia is in rough shape. As the hardware company scrambles to replace its CEO and co-founder, Jensen Huang, after a series of scandals, it seems the needs of lowly gamers aren’t high on the priority list. Earlier this year, the company ran into controversy over its ugly generative AI-fueled DLSS 5 technology. If you need more evidence that Nvidia is focused on cranking out chips for generative AI rather than supporting its gaming hardware, the company has failed to fix a bug reportedly capable of damaging users’ PCs by writing excessive amounts of data to solid-state drives (SSDs).

The bug was found in the last two driver updates for Nvidia graphics cards — specifically, the 621.14 and 621.24 updates for the Nvidia GeForce Game Ready Driver. The 621.24 update went live on Nov. 12. As first reported by TroubleGrid, the bug causes constant writes (saving data) on a user’s SSD, degrading the hardware while potentially causing stutters and mouse pointer hitches. Specifically, the bug apparently causes NVDisplay.Container.exe to write an excessive amount of information to an Nvidia log file, resulting in as much as 30 GB being written per hour.

While this executable already wrote to log files before the most recent driver update, versions 621.14 and 621.24 can cause dramatically more data to be written. It usually takes years for an SSD to degrade naturally, but the excessive data logged by the Nvidia bug could speed up this process. The bug seems to be causing performance issues for some users as data is written in the background. The easiest fix is to downgrade your graphics card driver to version 621.01, which is the last known patch that didn’t have the problem.

Users have reported the bug on most major PC gaming subreddits, and they’re not happy. “Trying to destroy our computers so we stop hoarding all their RAM,” one user said, referencing a now-infamous quote from OpenAI CEO Sam Altman. “AMD and NVIDIA releasing firmware that prematurely kills SSD’s when SSD’s have become 7x more expensive than they were 18 months ago [thinking emoji],” another wrote, conspiratorially implying that these bugs were deliberately created to damage users’ computers and make them buy expensive new SSDs. “Makes you wonder if it’s AI code incompetence or a concerted effort to kill personal computing,” someone else replied.

As this comment suggests, some have suggested that the bug may be a byproduct of Nvidia’s increasing use of AI-generated code. Earlier this year, Huang said he wanted his developers to use AI chatbots and agents more often to help with programming. In that context, this bug may be a Hanlon’s razor situation rather than a conspiracy: "Never attribute to malice that which is adequately explained by stupidity."

Ugly AI graphics and a 50% performance hit, what's not to love?

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

You Can Now Download and Print GTA 6's Official Leonida Map

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you miss the days of unfolding a paper map before heading into a new GTA game, Rockstar has something for you. The official map of Leonida is now available to download for free, and there's a print-ready version for anyone who wants a physical copy.

Rockstar has unveiled The Goodtime State – Vice City Collection, a special bundle packed with physical merchandise celebrating the game's setting.

Rockstar added the map to its GTA 6 website on November 19, the same day the game launched. You can also download the game's cover art as a poster, so there are actually two things worth grabbing if you've got a printer handy.

Head to the official GTA 6 media page and look for the section labeled 'Leonida Map & Cover Art Poster.' Select 'View Options' to open the downloads. Rockstar has separate choices for the Leonida map and cover art, including printable versions of both.

Pick 'Leonida Map - Printable' if you want to put it on paper, then use the download button. There's a regular map download as well, which is handy if you just want to keep a copy on your phone, tablet, or second monitor while you play.

The map gives you a proper look at the shape of Leonida, from Vice City's dense roads to the waterways, coastlines, and smaller areas further inland. If you've spent the last few years comparing trailer shots and fan-made maps, being able to look at Rockstar's official version is a pretty nice change.

If you want the entire thing rather than a cropped section, choose a matching paper size or ask a print shop to scale it to fit. The print-ready download is the version to use for that.

The other download is the game's cover art poster. Rockstar has made it available in a print-ready format too, so you can hang it up instead of just using it as a desktop wallpaper.

There's a bit of GTA history behind the appeal. Physical copies of GTA 3, Vice City, San Andreas, GTA 4, and GTA 5 all came with paper maps. Even spin-offs like Liberty City Stories, Vice City Stories, and Episodes from Liberty City followed that tradition. You could spend ages looking over the streets and place names before you'd actually explored them in-game.

I've lost the map that came with my copy of GTA: Episodes from Liberty City, and I'm sure some of you have misplaced yours too. Even the maps we've managed to keep aren't necessarily in great shape. Leaving them folded inside a game case for years can damage the paper along the creases. Having an official file you can print again is a pretty good solution to that.

You can obviously save the new map and check it on another screen, but printing it is half the appeal. Having something physical to look at while you explore Leonida feels a lot closer to those older releases.

You can obviously save the new map and check it on another screen, but printing it is half the appeal.

I can already see the Leonida map and GTA 6 cover art decorating plenty of fans' rooms. I'd be surprised if a fair few people didn't end up printing both.

If you'd rather have a professionally printed copy, Rockstar has another option coming. In its November 3 announcement, the company said a double-sided physical poster would be sold through the Rockstar Store shortly after launch.

That version will cost $0.99 on its own, or come free with another Rockstar Store purchase. Rockstar hasn't given an exact date for when it will go on sale, though, so don't confuse that upcoming physical item with the digital files you can download right now.

Boxed copies of GTA 6 are available, but as DualShockers previously reported, they contain download codes rather than discs. Reports on the PS5 edition also confirm that the case includes an artwork poster, not the traditional fold-out map. Whether Rockstar will ever bundle a map with a later boxed release is still unclear.

It would've been even better if Rockstar had confirmed that a physical Leonida map would come with the game, like it did with the older releases. Still, making high-resolution printable versions of both the map and cover art available for free is a good move. And unlike an old folded map, you can always print a fresh copy if yours gets damaged.

There's something nice about having an official paper map beside you while playing GTA, and now anyone who wants one can print it without having to track down a boxed copy.

GTA 6 features a completely rebuilt dynamic wind and atmospheric system designed to bring the tropical world of Leonida to life like never before.

Ripple’s Brad Garlinghouse Says Crypto Markets Could Rebound After Anthropic Goes Public

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse believes the recent crypto market sell-off stems largely from investors rotating capital into high-profile initial public offerings. The Ripple boss and crypto billionaire anticipates a recovery once Anthropic’s massive listing is fully absorbed.

Speaking to CNBC on November 19th, Garlinghouse drew a direct parallel to SpaceX’s public debut earlier in the year. He explained that when major IPOs generate intense demand, investors often sell liquid assets such as Bitcoin and XRP to free up cash for share subscriptions.

The same pattern, he said, unfolded ahead of Anthropic’s offering. After SpaceX completed its listing, crypto markets staged a notable rebound. Garlinghouse expects a similar bounce this time.

Anthropic, the artificial-intelligence company behind the Claude chatbot family, has priced its IPO at $83 per share, above the previously marketed range of $70 to $78.

The deal ranks among the largest IPOs in history, raising tens of billions of dollars and valuing the company in the trillion-dollar range, and shares are set to begin trading on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

Such blockbuster offerings typically attract enormous institutional and retail interest, temporarily diverting capital from other risk assets. Once the primary allocation is complete and secondary-market trading stabilizes, some of that capital often flows back into crypto and other liquid markets.

Bitcoin had slipped near $62,000 on Wednesday before recovering above $63,000 early Thursday. Garlinghouse framed the dip as a temporary capital-rotation effect rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in digital assets. He also expressed confidence that Anthropic’s shares would trade strongly given the strong demand and pricing above the range.

Looking further ahead, Garlinghouse remained optimistic about blockchain’s long-term role. He predicted that within roughly a decade, blockchain technology will be deeply embedded across global financial infrastructure, much as artificial intelligence has already transformed multiple industries.

His comments highlight how large technology IPOs can create short-term pressure on crypto while potentially reinforcing longer-term narratives around innovation and capital markets.

Rockstar Devs Concerned About Layoffs Despite GTA 6 Success

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

GTA 6 is here, and the open world of Leonida is ready for fans to explore. It’s still early days, but GTA 6 is on track to be one of the biggest games ever. It has tied for the best-reviewed game of all time, already generated $1.8 billion in gross revenue and counting, and broken Twitch’s viewership record in just 30 minutes.

The reviews for Grand Theft Auto 6 are in and it's now tied for the best-reviewed video game of all time, with Nintendo's greatest game matching it.

Amid all that success, you would think that GTA 6 developers are now able to kick back and relax, soaking in the excitement of sharing their work with the world. However, even though Rockstar Games hasn't revealed any layoffs, it remains a worry that looms over those who worked on the game.

In an opinion piece published in The Guardian, Animation Programmer at Rockstar Games, Nestor Uriarte, expressed concerns about job security and the pressures developers face.

He says that “while the highs of a game release like this can be incredible,” workers in the video game industry have also become accustomed to “less welcome side effects.” This covers both sides of development, as Uriarte mentions “intense overtime in the buildup and mass redundancies in the aftermath.”

Rockstar Games is one of many studios in previous years to face allegations of “crunch culture,” essentially requiring developers to work an unhealthy amount of overtime in the lead-up to a game’s launch date.

Uriarte believes that “with a strong union we could make sure there is no return to this system. We have already seen the introduction of some overtime pay, and now we want to keep on pushing to ensure that every hour worked in our industry is an hour paid.”

In the middle of the record-breaking release of GTA 6, Rockstar Games employees in the UK are voting on whether or not they want to unionize. The ballot, which will be voted on by approximately 1,600 staff, will close on November 26, 2026. The results of the vote should be announced shortly after voting closes.

Amid all that success, you would think that GTA 6 developers are now able to kick back and relax

Uriarte says that after the launch of GTA 6, Rockstar Games employees "have had no indication that layoffs are coming, but it’s hard not to worry about what the future holds.” He argues that the state of the industry means nobody is safe, “even my colleagues and I, working on the biggest game of all time, are worried about getting laid off. But the truth is that workers across the sector are helpless to resist the waves of redundancies crashing around us, until we get organised.”

Another threat to game development is the rise of AI. Uriarte claims, “Rockstar has said it is committed to preserving jobs and it has been clear that our games will continue to be made by people.” However, the push to secure union recognition would grant employees more of a say in that future.

Layoffs in the video game industry are commonplace, with Civilization studio, Firaxis Games, being the latest to slash its headcount. Other notable redundancies this year include EA shutting down BioWare, and the mass XBOX layoffs as part of the company’s restructuring process, among many others.

Although no layoffs seem to be on the cards at Rockstar Games, if they were to occur, it would call into question the sustainability of game development if a studio with such a huge release needs to let staff go.

GTA 6 removes the ability solicit, a GTA feature since 2001. Rockstar says the decision supports Jason and Lucia’s story.

JOYY (JOYY) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ad tech and commerce drove growth, while FX losses weighed on results.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 19, 2026 at 12:40PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

JOYY (JOYY -3.58%) reported third quarter results that demonstrated the continued execution of its multi-engine growth strategy, led by high-double-digit expansion in its advertising and smart commerce segments. Management reported that social entertainment remained a cornerstone of the business, achieving year-over-year growth in live streaming revenue across all four geographical areas. The company stated that its third-party advertising network is scaling toward a long-term revenue target of $1 billion, while its e-commerce platform is on track to reach operational breakeven by 2028. Total net revenues increased 15.6% for the quarter, and the company maintained a high pace of capital return through both dividends and its $600 million share repurchase program.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, thank you for standing by and welcome to JOYY Inc.'s Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. [Operator Instructions] I'd now like to hand the conference over to your host today, Xinyuan Liu, the company's Head of Investor Relations. Please go ahead, Xinyuan.

Xinyuan Liu: Thank you, operator. Hello, everyone. Welcome to JOYY's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. Joining us today are Ms. Ting Li, Chairperson and CEO of JOYY; and Mr. Fuyong Liu, Vice President of Finance. I will first read the prepared remarks on behalf of Ms. Li, followed by remarks from Mr. Liu. Both of them will be available to answer your questions during the Q&A session. The financial results and webcast of this conference call are available on the IR website, ir.joyy.com. Please note that today's call contains forward-looking statements made under the safe harbor provisions of the U.S. Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995.

Forward-looking statements are subject to risks and uncertainties that may cause actual results to differ materially from our current expectations. For detailed discussions of these risks and uncertainties, please refer to our latest annual report on Form 20-F and other documents filed with the SEC. Please also note that JOYY's earnings press release and this conference call include disclosures of GAAP and non-GAAP financial measures. A reconciliation of these non-GAAP measures to GAAP measures is included in today's earnings press release. All figures referenced on today's call are in U.S. dollars, unless otherwise noted. With that, I will now read the prepared remarks on behalf of Ms. Li. Hello, everyone. I'm Ting Li. Thank you for joining us today.

This quarter, we took another solid step toward becoming a global technology company with multiple growth engines. During the quarter, social entertainment remained on a steady course, while ad tech and smart commerce continued their strong momentum, driving solid top-line growth for the group. Notably, our third-party advertising business, BIGO Audience Network, continued to scale rapidly while also improving its margin, further demonstrating the effectiveness of our multi-engine growth strategy. In the third quarter, we generated total net revenues of $625 million, up 15.6% year-over-year and 5.7% quarter-over-quarter. Social entertainment revenue was $441 million, up 8.8% year-over-year.

BIGO Ads, including both first-party and third-party businesses, generated $146 million in revenue, up 39.6% year-over-year, with our third-party advertising business, BIGO Audience Network, sustaining strong growth of 52.3% year-over-year. Top-line revenue reached $37.8 million, up 24.7% year-over-year. The contribution from non-live streaming revenue further increased to 32.8% of total revenues this quarter. Non-GAAP operating income reached $46.6 million, up 14.6% year-over-year. Non-GAAP EBITDA reached $55.7 million, up 10.1% year-over-year. Operating cash flow for the quarter was $102.2 million. As of September 30, 2026, we held $2.94 billion in net cash. Supported by our strong cash flow and solid financial position, we continue to return capital to our shareholders for their long-term support through cash dividends and share repurchases.

As of November 13, we have returned a total of $536 million to shareholders this year. As we enter the final quarter of 2026, we remain focused not only on finishing the year with strong execution, but also on what comes next. I would like to take this opportunity to share our strategic priorities and business outlook for 2027 and the medium to long term. Over the past few years, we have continued to execute our multi-engine growth strategy, strengthening our core social entertainment business while developing ad tech and smart commerce as 2 new growth engines. Following our investments and business model validation over the past few years, the results of this strategy are becoming increasingly evident.

Looking ahead to 2027, we believe JOYY will enter an important phase as we advance our multi-engine growth strategy and translate our progress into stronger operating results. Social entertainment will remain a strategic cornerstone of our business, while ad tech and smart commerce emerging as key growth drivers for the group. In social entertainment, we will continue to execute our disciplined and efficiency-focused operating strategy, strengthening our core competence through operational excellence and product innovation. We will leverage our existing product modules, technology infrastructure, and localized operating capabilities to improve the efficiency of new product development and new market expansion, and drive continued growth from our new product portfolio.

We believe we are well-positioned to maintain steady growth while further enhancing the profitability and cash flow generation of our social entertainment business. Turning to BIGO Ads. A year ago, I expressed our confidence in building the platform into an important player in the third-party ad tech industry. Over the past year, BIGO Ads has made strong progress in daily average gross revenue, and we completed integrations with 3 of the world's leading mediation platforms and continue to expand our global traffic network and advertiser reach. It was also ranked in multiple regions and app categories in the 2026 AppsFlyer Performance Index.

These developments demonstrate BIGO Ads' growing competitive advantages in the global programmatic advertising market and further strengthen our confidence in achieving this goal. In 2027, we will continue to build BIGO Ads into an AI-powered performance advertising platform across multiple channels and industry verticals worldwide, scaling the business while reinforcing its long-term competitiveness. On the supply side, we will further expand our web traffic coverage and strengthen our capabilities across both web and app traffic, offering advertisers cross-channel solutions to meet conversion goals within different verticals. On the demand side, we will continue to expand across verticals, with a focus on attracting more advertising spend from web-based lead generation and e-commerce, while continuing to grow in-app demand.

These efforts help us broaden our advertiser base and attract more spending on our platform. On the technology side, as advertising demand expands across a broader range of verticals, including web-based lead generation, e-commerce, and gaming, we will further develop our multi-objective and multi-scenario modeling capabilities to improve the accuracy of conversion predictions across verticals. In addition, we will explore applying scaling approaches used in large language models and strengthen our sequence modeling capabilities. We are gradually building foundation model capabilities for advertising. As these capabilities develop, we expect better campaign performance to attract more advertiser spending and improve traffic monetization, further reinforce the platform's growth flywheel.

For Shopline, we will continue to attract high-quality brand merchants and strengthen our presence in key markets while enhancing our omnichannel e-commerce infrastructure and value-added services to optimize merchant operations across markets and channels. We will also expand merchants' adoption of SHOPLINE Payments and deepen its usage, integrating payment services more closely into merchants' transaction flows to drive sustainable growth in value-added services revenue. Meanwhile, we will continue to advance our AI Commerce initiatives to help merchants reach customers through emerging AI-powered shopping channels and further strengthen Shopline's value as an omnichannel e-commerce infrastructure platform.

As our merchant base expands, transaction volume growth, and value-added services penetration increases, we expect to realize greater economies of scale and operating leverage from our existing product and technology platform. With increasingly clear growth paths across our 3 business lines, we believe the group will sustain solid revenue growth in 2027. We also expect greater scale, a stronger earnings mix, and improved operating efficiency to drive further growth in operating profitability. At the same time, shareholder returns remain an integral part of our long-term capital allocation strategy. Supported by our robust cash flow generation and solid capital position, we will continue to return capital to shareholders for their long-term support through share repurchases and cash dividends.

Looking ahead, we will continue to pursue business growth and shareholder returns in tandem. As we strengthen profitability and cash flow generation, we are committed to ensuring that our shareholders share in the benefits of our multi-engine growth strategy. Next, I will walk through our third quarter results and share our outlook for the future. In the third quarter, social entertainment continued to deliver steady growth with a further acceleration in year-over-year growth momentum. Our global average mobile MAUs reached 278.8 million, up 4.7% year-over-year. Driven by strong user engagement and organic growth, our instant messaging product further increased its contribution to total MAUs. Both average time spent per user and the retention rate for the IM product increased year-over-year.

In the third quarter, live streaming revenue was $420 million, up 8.1% year-over-year and 4.3% quarter-over-quarter. Supported by effective refined operations, core live streaming paying users grew 8.1% year-over-year and 4.9% quarter-over-quarter. Bigo Live, our flagship product, continued to deliver steady revenue growth both year-over-year and quarter-over-quarter, supported by our ongoing efforts in content operations, streamer ecosystem, and AI applications. In content operations, we continued to build on Bigo Live's strengths as a global social platform to improve cross-regional content distribution and social interaction for users. We further refined our cross-regional content distribution algorithm, expanded live translation coverage, and improved speech recognition in noisy environments, making it easier for users to interact across languages.

These improvements drove continued growth in cross-regional gifting among users in developed markets. In the streamer ecosystem, we continued to invest in our community by refining incentives for new streamers and strengthening support for promising talents. In particular, we improved onboarding and guidance to help newly signed streamers successfully launch their first live streams. These efforts contributed to a 4.7 percentage point increase in the share of newly signed streamers completing their first live streams from last quarter, bolstering our high-quality content pipeline. We continued to integrate AI into live stream interactions and virtual gifting, expanding our AI-generated interactive content and gifts to create a more personalized user experience.

In September, the share of total virtual gift consumption attributable to AI-generated interactive virtual gifts increased further to 35.4%. At the same time, our new voice product portfolio continued to grow rapidly. In the third quarter, revenue from these new products more than tripled year-over-year and increased around 30% sequentially, continuing to serve as a meaningful complement to our social entertainment growth. Our current fourth quarter guidance implies mid-single-digit year-over-year growth for social entertainment revenue. We expect that our social entertainment business will continue to grow steadily and contribute sustainable cash flow to the group going forward. Moving on to BIGO Ads.

In the third quarter, we continued to expand our global traffic network, diversifying our advertiser verticals and demand mix, and enhance ad delivery through algorithm improvements. As a result, the third quarter advertising business reached $146 million, up 39.6% year-over-year and 9% quarter-over-quarter. Our third-party advertising business, BIGO Audience Network, grew 52.3% year-over-year and 9.2% quarter-over-quarter. On the supply side, we continued to expand our global developer ecosystem and broaden our high-quality traffic coverage. In the third quarter, SDK traffic grew 55.6% year-over-year. In October, BIGO Ads officially integrated with Google AdMob Mediation. Developers can now seamlessly access the BIGO Ads SDK using Google AdMob Mediation and tap into demand from advertisers around the world.

This integration opens up new opportunities for us to expand and diversify our high-quality traffic supply. On the demand side, web-based demand within BIGO Ads' third-party advertising business, primarily from lead generation and e-commerce, grew at an accelerated pace, increasing by around 328% year-over-year and 30% quarter-over-quarter, and serving as a key growth driver of BIGO Ads this quarter. We continued to deepen our presence in key verticals by building stronger local advertiser relationships and accumulating more conversion data. These insights helped us further optimize our algorithms and ad delivery solutions for different web-based verticals.

As a result, we strengthened our ability to attract new advertisers and capture more ad budgets while expanding our lead generation advertising into more high-value sub-verticals. Meanwhile, IAA demand maintained healthy momentum. On the technology side, we continued to strengthen our data and AI-driven algorithm capabilities to improve the precision and efficiency of advertising delivery. As our cross-channel attribution systems improved and advertiser conversion data accumulated, we broadened our model training inputs, improving prediction of conversion probability and user lifetime value. We also upgraded our model architecture to support different conversion objectives and traffic types, improving accuracy and accelerating iteration.

Meanwhile, we continue to refine our bidding strategies to better assess the value of each advertising impression and allocate advertiser budgets more effectively. These improvements helped advertisers meet their ROI targets while making our traffic acquisition and monetization more efficient. As our business continued to scale and our algorithm and operating efficiency continued to improve, our third-party advertising business also achieved a modest margin improvement this quarter. We will continue to pursue growth opportunities in third-party advertising, scaling the business, and laying the groundwork for larger profit contribution in the years ahead. We remain committed to our goal of reaching USD 1 billion in revenue from our third-party advertising, BIGO Audience Network, in 2028. Turning to Shopline.

We continued to prioritize the acquisition of high-quality brand merchants in the third quarter. Shopline revenue reached $37.8 million, up 24.7% year-over-year and 10% quarter-over-quarter. As brand merchants scale and expand across multiple sales channels, they need more than an online storefront. They need a platform that brings transactions and customer data together from different sales channels and streamlines their day-to-day operations. To meet their needs, Shopline continued to strengthen its integrated offerings across online store setup, payments, marketing, and customer management. Backed by local expertise and support across markets, Shopline helps merchants streamline their operations and boost their cross-channel efficiency. In the cross-border business, we focused on established brands with meaningful scale and an existing overseas presence.

We enhanced our service offerings and local support to help these merchants migrate to our platform, manage operation across markets, and process cross-border transactions more efficiently. In the third quarter, revenue from cross-border merchants maintained strong momentum, growing 77.8% year-over-year. To support our growing base of brand merchants, we continued to enhance SHOPLINE Payments with more local payment options, stronger multi-currency payment support, and improved transaction management. These enhancements help merchants easily and efficiently manage cross-border payments, making payment services an important driver of cross-border growth. Leveraging our existing merchant base and platform capabilities, we can scale value-added services, including payments, without a proportionally increase in sales and R&D expenses.

As revenue and gross profit continue to increase, we expect Shopline's economies of scale to become more evident, driving steady improvement in its operating profitability. In AI Commerce, our strategy revolves around helping merchants capture demand from AI channels and improve their operational efficiency. As AI agents change how customers discover and purchase products, we continue to strengthen our capabilities and expand integrations with the broader AI ecosystem. While volume from AI channels is still in an early stage, adoption is building quickly. In the third quarter, page views and order volume from AI channels grew nearly 500% and 550% year-over-year, respectively.

Notably, AI is demonstrating distinct advantages in categories such as consumer electronics and furniture, where shoppers weigh multiple options before buying. In these categories, AI can better interpret consumers' needs, recommend relevant products, and guide them to a purchase. To improve merchants' productivity, we rolled out our AI-powered commerce assistant, Shopline Omi, spanning product management, customer segmentation, promotional events, multilingual support, and detailed store performance analysis. These tools bring AI into more day-to-day workflows, helping merchants manage their businesses more efficiently. We also expanded integrations with leading AI platforms and agents and enhanced our AI tools as well as interfaces for developers.

These initiatives help merchants adapt to an evolving commerce landscape and could open new avenues for Shopline's medium to long-term growth. Our current fourth quarter guidance implies that Shopline will continue to deliver more than 20% year-over-year revenue growth. We remain confident that Shopline will continue to enhance its operating performance and achieve its breakeven target in 2028 as planned. In closing, 2027 will be a key year for executing our multi-engine growth strategy and translating our progress into stronger operating results. By strengthening each of our 3 business lines and deepening synergies across our ecosystem, we aim to build a more diversified and resilient business that delivers sustainable growth and lasting shareholders value.

With that, I will now hand the call over to Fuyong Liu, our Vice President of Finance, to walk through our financial results in detail.

Fuyong Liu: Thanks, Ms. Li. Hello, everyone. Let me begin with some headline numbers. For the third quarter of 2026, our total net revenues came in at $625 million, up 15.6% year-over-year and 5.7% quarter-over-quarter. non-GAAP EBITDA reached $56 million, an increase of 10.1% year-over-year and broadly flat on a sequential basis. We generated $102 million of operating cash flow in this quarter and ended September with net cash of $2.94 billion. We have stayed proactive on capital return throughout the year. Year-to-date, through November 30, 2026, we have returned a total of $536 million to shareholders, comprising $260 million in cash dividends and $320 million in share repurchase.

I will now dive deeper into our detailed financial performance for the third quarter. Social entertainment revenues were $441 million, up 8.8% year-over-year and 4.3% sequentially. In particular, live streaming revenues increased to $420 million, up 8.1% year-over-year and 4.3% quarter-over-quarter, maintaining solid growth momentum. Core live streaming paying users grew 8.1% year-over-year and 4.8% quarter-over-quarter, while ARPPU stood at $219, largely flat sequentially. Encouragingly, we also delivered year-over-year live streaming revenue growth across all 4 of our geographical areas worldwide, which are developed countries and regions, the Middle East, Mainland and Southeast Asia, and others. BIGO Ads revenue was $146 million, an increase of 39.6% year-over-year and 9% sequentially.

Our third-party advertising business, BIGO Audience Network, maintained robust growth, up 52.3% year-over-year and 9.2% quarter-over-quarter. On the supply side, SDK traffic scale grew 55.6% year-over-year. On the demand side, web-based demand, mainly leads generation and e-commerce, grew around 328% year-over-year and around 30% quarter-over-quarter, while IAA demand also continued to grow at a healthy pace. With greater scale and continued improvements in our algorithm and operating efficiency, our third-party advertising operating margin edged up this quarter. Shopline's revenue reached $38 million, growing 24.7% year-over-year and 10% quarter-over-quarter, primarily driven by continued merchant adoption and deeper penetration of value-added service. Cross-border merchants continued to be a key driver, with revenue from this cohort up 77.8% year-over-year.

Group's gross profit was $217 million in this quarter, up 12.5% year-over-year and 7.7% quarter-over-quarter, with gross margin at 34.8%, expanding on a sequential basis. Social entertainment's gross margin improved quarter-over-quarter as we continued to improve user engagement and monetization. BIGO Ads gross margin improved quarter-over-quarter also, reflecting the margin improvement in third-party advertising. Shopline's gross margin was down quarter-over-quarter as lower-margin value-added service, particularly payments and marketing, accounted for a large scale of revenue. While these value-added service carry a lower gross margin than subscriptions, as we shared previously, they typically require less incremental sales and R&D investment to scale.

We remain confident that this revenue mix shift will continue to drive Shopline's operating leverage and support its path to long-term profitability. Our operating expenses for the quarter were $204 million, up 17.2% year-over-year and 8.4% quarter-on-quarter. Sales and marketing expenses were higher year-over-year, reflecting our spending to support revenue growth. G&A expenses were higher year-over-year also, primarily due to higher impairment losses and share-based compensation expenses. R&D expenses were down year-over-year, driven by operational synergies across business units, while we strategically redirected R&D resources toward BIGO Ads. non-GAAP operating income was $47 million, up 14.6% year-over-year.

Non-GAAP net income from continuing operations attributable to controlling interest and common shareholders of JOYY was $67 million, down 7.4% year-over-year, and represented a non-GAAP net margin of 10.7%. The year-over-year decline was mainly due to non-operating items, mainly because higher FX losses. Turning to our business outlook, with continued momentum across social entertainment, BIGO Ads, and Shopline, we expect total net revenues for the fourth quarter of 2026 to be between $650 million and $669 million, implying year-over-year growth of 11.7% to 14.9%.

For the full year of 2026, we remain confident in delivering solid revenue growth at the group level, and we reaffirm our expectation that the group's full year 2026 non-GAAP operating income will grow around 20% year-over-year. Before I conclude, I would like to spend a minute on 2027 from a financial perspective. Across our business segments, social entertainment has returned to growth and continues to generate healthy cash flow. BIGO Ads is a fast-scaling profitable advertising platform with improving operating efficiency. Shopline is expanding steadily toward its 2028 break-even timeline. Looking ahead to 2027, we expect the group will sustain a solid revenue growth, accompanied by expanded group non-GAAP operating profit.

We will stay disciplined in managing costs and expenses, allocate resources more efficiently, and remain committed to strong capital return. To summarize, we delivered another strong quarter with all 3 business segments achieving healthy and high-quality expansion. We firmly believe our shares remain substantially undervalued, and we expect to continue actively executing our repurchase program. Looking ahead, we remain fully committed to executing our multi-engine strategy, driving profitable growth, and serving long-term value for our shareholders. That concludes our prepared remarks. Operator, we would now like to open up the call to questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Your first question comes from Xueqing Zhang with CICC. Please go ahead.

Xueqing Zhang: [Foreign Language]Congratulations on the strong quarter. My question about BIGO Ads, following officially integration with Google AdMob, how much incremental traffic do you expect to see? Can management also share the outlook for the third-party advertising revenue growth for full year 2026 and 2027?

Ting Li: [Foreign Language] Thank you for your question. in October, we officially completed our integration with Google AdMob. Developers can now seamlessly access BIGO Ads SDK by using Google AdMob Mediation, which allows them to tap into massive demand from global premium advertisers. I think this integration also creates new opportunities for us to further expand our supply of high-quality traffic and reach a broader range of application scenarios. To date, we have completed integrations with 3 major programmatic advertising mediation platforms, including AppLovin MAX, Unity LevelPlay, and Google AdMob. This marks an important milestone in the development of BIGO Ads traffic ecosystem. It has only been a little more than a month since the official integration with Google AdMob.

While the initial data trends are encouraging, it is still in an early stage, and the contribution to our total revenue remains relatively small for now. We will continue to monitor its performance and share further updates with all of you when appropriate. In the third quarter, our third-party advertising revenue grew by 52.3% year-on-year, driven primarily by expanded traffic supply, rapid growth in web-based lead generation and e-commerce advertising demand, as well as improvements in our model architecture, algorithm accuracy, and iteration efficiency. For the full year 2026, we are confident that third-party advertising revenue will grow by nearly 60% year-on-year, above our previous guidance of around 50%.

We expect our advertising business to maintain rapid growth in 2027 and reach our goal of USD 1 billion in third-party advertising revenue in 2028. Next question, please.

Operator: Your next question comes from Chi Tsang with HSBC. Please go ahead.

Chi Tsang: [Foreign Language] Congrats on the very strong guidance for fourth quarter. I want to ask about your views on 2027 revenue and profit outlook. Thank you.

Fuyong Liu: [Foreign Language] Thank you for your question. We are still in the process of developing our detailed operating plans for 2027, so we are not yet able to provide specific guidance right now. However, based on the momentum across our 3 business lines, we are confident in our growth prospects in 2027. On the revenue side, our social entertainment business has delivered solid growth over the past several quarters, supported by our disciplined and efficiency-focused operating strategy and product innovation. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027.

For BIGO Ads, as we continue to expand our global traffic supply and advertiser coverage, develop advertiser verticals such as lead generation, e-commerce, and gaming, and iterate on our model capabilities, we expect this business to maintain rapid growth in 2027. As we just mentioned, we remain confident in our goal of reaching USD 1 billion in third-party advertising revenue in 2028. For Shopline, cross-border business will remain a key growth driver in 2027. With continued product enhancements and growing adoption of value-added services such as payments, we believe Shopline will continue to deliver rapid revenue growth.

On the profitability side, we expect social entertainment to continue to generate solid profit, BIGO Ads to further improve its margin through rapidly growing revenue, and Shopline to continue narrowing its operating losses. As a result, we expect our group-level non-GAAP EBITDA to maintain solid growth in 2027. Heading into 2027, we will enter an important phase as we advance our multi-engine growth strategy, and we remain confident in our ability to deliver long-term sustainable growth. Next question, please.

Operator: Your next question comes from Thomas Chong with Jefferies. Please go ahead.

Thomas Chong: [Foreign Language] My question is about the live streaming business. Can management comment about the rationale for the good growth momentum over the past couple of quarters? And what are the key drivers? Thank you.

Ting Li: [Foreign Language] Thank you for your question. Our live streaming business has delivered sequential revenue growth since the second quarter of 2025, and both year-over-year and sequential revenue growth since the second quarter of 2026. MAUs and paying users have also continued to increase. This reflects our efforts in these areas. Firstly, in content operations, we have continued to build on our efficiency-focused operations, product innovation, and years of localized operating experience to refine cross-regional content distribution algorithm and improve the cross-regional content consumption experience. In the third quarter, the cross-regional consumption of Bigo Live accounted for 1/3 of total live streaming consumption.

At the same time, we have continued to expand the coverage of live translation and improve the accuracy of multi-speaker voice recognition in noisy environments, making it easier for users to communicate across languages. Secondly, within our streamer ecosystem, we have continued to enhance our development and incentive programs for new streamers, attracting more high-quality new streamers to our platform. Thirdly, our portfolio of new voice products has maintained rapid growth, providing additional momentum for the overall live streaming business. In addition, we continue to use AI to enhance live streaming interactives and paying experience. In September, AI-generated interactive virtual gifts on Bigo Live accounted for 35% of our total virtual gifting consumption, demonstrating a further increase. Next question, please.

Operator: Your next question comes from Sardonna Fong with UBS. Please go ahead.

Sardonna Fong: [Foreign Language] Congrats on a very solid 3Q results and outlook. My question is on Shopline, especially on AI Commerce. How is the contribution of it to Shopline's growth? As AI emerges as a new traffic channel, could management share the latest progress Shopline has made in AI Commerce and also the outlook going forward?

Ting Li: [Foreign Language] Thank you for your question. For AI Commerce, we see AI emerging as a new channel for consumers to discover products and complete purchases, creating new traffic and transaction opportunities for brand merchants. At the same time, we are developing AI tools for merchants and developers to support the long-term development of AI Commerce. For merchants, AI-powered commerce assistant, Shopline Omi, has been rolling out capabilities across product management, multilingual intelligent Q&A, in-depth store performance analysis, and merchant operations. These tools can help merchants optimize product titles, descriptions, inventory, and pricing. They can also analyze revenue, traffic sources, and conversion performance, helping merchants identify issues, receive recommendations, and improve day-to-day operation efficiency and conversion rates.

For developers, we have made an MCP server available. Developers can use Shopline's documentation and related services within mainstream AI coding tools to develop or customize applications more efficiently, further lowering the bar to learn it. For consumers, AI has shown particular potential in categories such as electronics and furniture, where purchase decisions can be complex and product specification can be difficult to navigate. AI can help understand consumers' needs, recommend suitable products, and facilitate conversions. We will continue to explore how these capabilities can make it easier for consumers to discover products and complete purchases. While AI channels still account for a relatively small share of traffic today, they are growing rapidly.

In the third quarter, page views and order volume from AI channels increased by nearly 500% and 550% year-over-year, respectively. We believe the changes AI is bringing to product discovery and transactions are well aligned with brands operating their own online stores. Looking ahead, Shopline will continue to enhance its product capabilities, helping merchants connect with AI traffic channels and improve operating efficiency through AI tools, while providing more comprehensive support for developers. We look forward to growing alongside the evolving AI ecosystem and capturing longer-term growth opportunities. Next question, please.

Operator: Your next question comes from Brian Gong with Citi. Please go ahead.

Brian Gong: [Foreign Language] My question is, our stock performance has been strong over the past few quarters, and under such circumstances, will JOYY continue to proactively do its shareholder return ahead? Thank you.

Fuyong Liu: [Foreign Language] Thank you for your question. Supported by our strong business fundamentals, ongoing shareholder returns, and the long-term support of our shareholders, our share price has continued to rise over the past few quarters in the past 2 years. However, we believe our current share price still does not fully reflect the intrinsic value of our company. Year to date, we have returned a total of USD 536 million to our shareholders, including approximately USD 320 million in share repurchases and USD 216 million in cash dividends, maintaining a strong pace of shareholder returns. Delivering consistent and meaningful returns to shareholders has always been an important part of our long-term capital allocation strategy.

Going forward, supported by our solid cash flow generation and ample financial resources, we will continue to return capital to shareholders through share repurchases and cash dividends in recognition of their longer-term support. As our operating performance continues to improve, we expect to create even more longer-term value for our shareholders.

Operator: There are no further questions. This conference is now concluded. Thank you for attending today's presentation. You may now disconnect.

This is technically the worst version of GTA 6 that we'll ever play

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.
