Still to come: PC performance, Online, patches, more graphics settings, and next-gen consoles.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I've been playing GTA 6 through the morning to try to see what the fuss is about, and now I totally get it. After mere hours in the game, I'm already coming to terms with the scale of it; not just the size of the map, but the attention to detail on every object and interaction. But I have to remind myself, this is sure to be the worst version of GTA 6 we'll ever play.

That sounds like an insult, but I mean the exact opposite. If this is where the game is at launch, at its most barebones, running on years-old consoles on their way out, then think of what it will be like at its peak.

I work for PC Gamer, so forgive my snobbery when I say the PC version should be far superior to what we're currently playing on consoles.

A part of that is the possible frame rate bump from running on modern PC hardware. The standard PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series S/X are powered by pretty outdated architectures by today's standards: RDNA 2 and Zen 2. You're looking at the Radeon RX 6000 series and Ryzen 3000 series for PC equivalents.

These chips have recently received support for more modern ML-powered FSR upscaling techniques from AMD, though Rockstar has only seen fit to enable FSR 4 on the Xbox Series S to help the underpowered console along. Sony also has AMD-powered upscaling tech for the PS5 Pro, but the standard PS5 doesn't get a whiff of that.

It's only really the PS5 Pro with a more modern GPU—loosely equivalent to an RX 7800 XT—that might temper expectations for how fast the game may run on a modern gaming PC. Rockstar has said the PlayStation 5 Pro wouldn't be capable of a steady 60 fps even with the more powerful hardware compared to the base model; however, hints at the sluggish CPU as perhaps the weak link. The PS5 Pro runs the same old eight-core CPU as the standard PS5, and if Rockstar's engine can make use of faster, more multithreaded CPUs with lots of cache, we should see a performance bump on PC.

That's not to exclude older gaming PCs. The Xbox Series S can cope with GTA 6, so should many gaming PCs of all levels. Notably so if Rockstar offers a range of upscaling options.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

It's ubiquitous upscaling that should make an even bigger difference to an eventual PC release and performance on all manner of PC. We know the game supports FSR 4.1, as both Sony and Microsoft are already using this as the foundation for their upscaling technologies on console, and Red Dead Redemption 2 offers both FSR and DLSS on PC. Since that game's release, we've seen the advent of frame generation technologies from both AMD and Nvidia, which could further increase how smooth GTA 6 feels on PC.

As a serial settings tweaker, I've been sorely disappointed by the sliders, or lack thereof, available to me in GTA 6 on the base PS5. I want levels to this stuff; an advanced menu for advanced men like me. I want to manage the foliage detail, draw distance, and ray-traced effects. Speaking of which, I'm sure we'll have the option to enable ray-traced shadows, currently exclusive to the PS5 Pro, and crank up the quality of most effects in the game on PC.

The non-RT shadows are one of the few noticeable drawbacks about the standard PS5 version of GTA 6 I'm currently playing. They can pop in and out from reflections, and there is a jagginess to them in places.

Is any game going to be modded more than GTA 6? Our Joshua has been busy modding GTA 5 to try to turn it alchemy-like into GTA 6 for months now, and he never ran out of mods to install. While he may have strayed from his original mission almost immediately, the message is clear: GTA 6 is going to have a seriously busy mod scene.

The game will be all the better for it, too. Not every mod is going to enable a roving band of militant furries (Joshua's words, and no need to mod those into GTA 6) or trap the player in a tiny car; I'm sure GTA 6 will actually be a better game for the efforts of modders. It just takes a PC version and some intrepid people to make it happen.

This one is easy; one of the reasons for GTA 5's long-lasting legacy is the combination of GTA Online and custom jobs via the creator. Neither of which are actually here for GTA 6 yet. So on a very basic level, and you don't have to be a PC gamer to agree on this point, GTA 6 is going to get a lot better for the eventual online mode. And there will be an online mode, right? Even if Rockstar hasn't technically announced one is on the way yet.

On the flipside, if you're constantly getting griefed by a guy that only plays GTA 6 all day and won't let you leave your house in GTA Online, perhaps you could argue the game was better when it was single-player only.

It pains me to admit it, but GTA 6 is not only going to eventually run better on PC. It might be the definitive version in my eyes, but console gamers do have a high-fidelity future ahead of them, too. Both Sony and Microsoft are planning new consoles that seem pretty likely to come out in roughly a year's time—providing the memory crisis doesn't ruin everything—and AMD is set to be providing more powerful silicon for these systems.

We're expecting to see the RDNA 5 architecture make its way into the next-gen consoles, paired with Zen 6 CPU cores of some size and description (these may be heterogeneous). That would put the consoles on a level playing field with PC components likely to launch around the same time (Zen 6 should be available on desktop by then, while perhaps a more limited launch for RDNA 5 by early 2028).

So that whole spiel about how current-gen consoles are years behind our fancy PCs won't get me so far once these new systems come out. I'll have to find a new stick to bash consoles with… er, not enough RGB LEDs? Anyways, the definitive GTA 6 experience on console is still yet to come—that'll be the version that's ready to make the most of these more capable units sometime next year or in 2028.

There are going to be a helluva lot of patches, upgrades, and DLCs to come for GTA 6. Remember, GTA V got a new first-person mode with the then-next-gen PS4 and Xbox One, so there's plenty of opportunity for genuine upgrades down the line.

Though, saying that, by recent PC standards (poor ports, game-breaking bugs, and more), this GTA 6 launch has been relatively issue-free. I mean, I've seen a couple of visual glitches and my colleagues have had some strange men falling out of the sky, but besides those open-world oddities you may expect of a game of this size and stature, it's been pretty smooth sailing so far.

I just hope the PC version can claim a similarly clean sweep for its eventual launch. So far, no word on when that might be.

Delta Air Lines CEO Called Wi-Fi Essential, Now Says Passengers Don’t Choose Airlines For It

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Delta Air Lines CEO Ed Bastian once called connectivity essential to daily life and promised an onboard experience resembling what passengers enjoy on the ground. Now, defending the decision to wait for Amazon Leo rather than install Starlink, he says customers do not choose airlines based on Wi-Fi. I understand the argument he is trying to make, but Delta spent years teaching passengers to expect more from their internet connection. Some of us listened.

Speaking with Fortune Editor-in-Chief Alyson Shontell at the Fortune 500 Innovation Forum on November 16, Bastian defended Delta’s connectivity strategy.

“This is about the total experience, and customers are not choosing airlines based on the Wi-Fi.”

He acknowledged enjoying Starlink himself, explained that Delta and SpaceX could not agree on a business model, and expressed confidence that Amazon could deliver a competitive alternative. Bastian also defended the existing Viasat product, describing it as “pretty darn close” to Starlink.

We can debate his last point, but his broader claim about how customers choose airlines strikes me as far too dismissive, especially considering what he has said before.

When Delta announced its free Wi-Fi initiative in January 2023, Bastian described connectivity in very different terms. Back then, he said:

“At work, at home and everywhere in between, connectivity is essential to daily life, and your journey on Delta should be no different.”

In the accompanying T-Mobile announcement, he added:

“The ability to stay connected while seeing the world is simply foundational.”

I agreed with him. Delta made fast, free Wi-Fi part of its premium proposition. The message was that passengers could expect a more useful and enjoyable flight because they could remain connected.

The airline has continued making that case. Its own explanation of why travelers should choose Delta includes fast, free Wi-Fi among the benefits it promotes.

Now that its primary competitor, United, is rapidly approaching a point in which it will have a superior internet product, the message sounds more like passengers should focus on everything else.

I tend to give people the benefit of the doubt and Bastian is no exception.

Saying connectivity is essential does not necessarily mean passengers will select the airline with the fastest connection. Bastian could reasonably argue that Delta already provides sufficiently good Wi-Fi and that another improvement in speed will not overcome differences in schedules, service, or loyalty.

But saying customers do not choose airlines based on Wi-Fi goes considerably further. Some certainly do. I am one of them. Delta cannot spend years promoting connectivity as part of what makes its product worth choosing, then expect no scrutiny when its CEO downplays connectivity as a reason to choose a competitor.

Perhaps Bastian has data showing that relatively few passengers have changed their booking habits. I would be interested in seeing it. His statement, however, leaves little room for customers whose behavior already contradicts it. He’s essentially inviting them to fly to United instead.

Not that I fly Delta often, but unlike some of my colleagues I’ve had very good experiences with Wi-Fi on Delta.

The Viasat Wi-Fi I have used on both American Airlines and Delta has generally been fine for the work I do. I can work on the blog, handle email, and complete other tasks without needing the fastest connection available.

Starlink or Viasat, I just need my connection to work and I should not have to wonder whether it will (one reason there are many aircraft on United, like the 757-300, that I avoid like the plague). If Viasat lets me accomplish what I need to accomplish, that flight remains productive and I’m a happy passenger. I am not going to pretend otherwise simply because I prefer Starlink.

But Starlink has raised my expectations. It is objectively faster and more responsive and it more closely resembles working from the ground…always. When I have comparable options, that influences my decision. I have already explained how I try to improve my odds of finding Starlink on United Airlines.

That does not mean I would accept any fare or routing to get it, but it means connectivity enters the calculation alongside departure time and price, particularly on a daytime flight. For someone whose work travels with him, several productive hours onboard have immense value…and I cannot relax on a plane if there is work to be done (that miserable United 777 flight from SFO-EWR comes to mind).

Bastian is right that passengers evaluate the total experience, but the internet connection is part of that experience and increasingly a critical part of the experience as passengers expectations evolve.

A good crew and comfortable seat are welcome and in some ways foundational, but so is being able to finish work before landing rather than spending the evening catching up instead of spending it with my family.

Different passengers will assign different values to those things. Someone taking a vacation may be delighted to disconnect. Someone traveling during the workday may be far more concerned about whether the connection will support the tasks waiting on a laptop. I do not expect Bastian to concede that every passenger should leave Delta for United, which is just a stupid argument. But recognizing that connectivity can influence a booking would hardly undermine the rest of Delta’s product…Bastian suddenly sounds defensive and tone deaf.

It would simply acknowledge how some customers use their time onboard…

I have defended the commercial logic behind Delta choosing Amazon Leo. An airline has legitimate reasons to consider control over its passenger interface and the broader business relationship when selecting a technology partner. We should also welcome competition for Starlink. Having another capable provider would be good for airlines and passengers.

But Delta plans to begin installing Leo on an initial 500 aircraft in 2028. That leaves time for competitors to establish new expectations, even if Amazon ultimately delivers an excellent product. As I discussed earlier this week, the failed launch carrying 40 Amazon satellites illustrates the execution risk involved in waiting for a network still being built, even if Delta has multiple back-up options.

Delta may have made the right long-term choice: only time will tell. But that does not mean passengers will ignore what competitors offer in the meantime and I’m fairly comfortable in saying Delta will lose a fair number of customers due to its Wi-Fi disparity with United.

Bastian once described connectivity as essential and foundational. He was right then, making his recent remarks puzzling, even when taken in context. I also concede that the Viasat service on American and Delta has generally worked well for my needs. This is not a case of Starlink being the only usable internet in the sky.

But reliable Wi-Fi now influences which flights I book, and Starlink increasingly gives me another reason to choose one departure over another and specifically, to choose United Airlines over others. Delta helped make connectivity something passengers expect. It should not be surprised when passengers begin choosing airlines based on who delivers it best.

Does Wi-Fi influence which airline or flight you book?

Google signs deal with Blue Origin to send satellite data centers into orbit

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This is part of Project Suncatcher, an experiment aimed at running AI systems in space.

Google has partnered up with Jeff Bezos' Blue Origin to deploy small satellite data centers into space, as part of its Project Suncatcher project. Each of these satellites will be equipped with "roughly the capacity of a data center rack" and will enter into a sun-synchronous orbit via heavy-lift New Glenn rockets.

The rocket will carry several Google tensor processing units, the company's proprietary AI chips, along with data center servers. The first launch isn't scheduled until early 2029, so we have a couple of years to see if this whole project even works.

Blue Origin and Google have entered into a launch services agreement for Project Suncatcher, Google's research moonshot exploring how an interconnected network of solar-powered satellites equipped with its AI chips, Tensor Processing Units (TPUs), could scale machine learning... pic.twitter.com/dUWuHR4Pla

— Blue Origin (@blueorigin) November 19, 2026

This is an experiment that Google calls a "long-term moonshot" to see if it's possible to put AI infrastructure in space by "tapping into the abundant solar power in orbit." The benefits of this would be massive. Data centers here on Earth are notorious hogs, with their snouts all over electricity grids, land and cooling systems.

The hope is that the harsh vacuum of the cosmos will be a better fit for the technology. After all, in space nobody can hear you make endless industrial noises and high-pitched whirs all day every day. This is a very real situation for folks living near data centers.

Google is far from the only company trying to kick the data center can straight into outer space. Elon Musk's SpaceX has been experimenting with the idea, though it may involve putting a gigantic catapult on the moon (really.) There are also some startups fiddling with the technology, like Starcloud.

Can this even work? We don't know yet, though there are some serious hurdles to overcome. Google did deploy its first AI server satellite back in October, but it was sent into low-Earth orbit and can only operate for 15 minutes at a time. Scaling up the affiliated cooling systems looks to be incredibly difficult.

That's because standard heat dissipation doesn't work in space, so it has to be released through pipes and panels rather than conventional cooling systems. There's also the matter of radiation. It's everywhere and causes something called "bit flip." This essentially flips the binary code from a one to a zero, or vice versa. Obviously, this would be a major issue for binary-coded systems.

Did I mention the actual launches? It's extremely expensive to send stuff to space. Just one Falcon 9 launch costs around $74 million and the maximum payload can barely cover the hardware for a small server room, let alone a hyperscale data center.

"Expanding from one satellite to a vast network of them that operate like a giant data center will take years and enormous funds," Brandon Lucia, a professor of electrical and computer engineering at Carnegie Mellon University, told The New York Times. "If you do this on a large scale, there are additional engineering problems."

Finally, there's a risk to us humans here on Earth. Scientists have been considering a theoretical scenario called Kessler syndrome for decades. This is when the density of objects in low-orbit gets so high and collisions become so commonplace that it creates a chain reaction of debris. We would be on the receiving end of that debris.

This theory was posited back when there were just under 2,000 items circling a low-Earth orbit. That number has since risen to over 47,000 and Elon Musk recently floated an idea to add millions of new satellites into the mix.

Meta’s AI agent Muse is mixing up conversations and exposing private data, report says

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Meta employees have raised concerns that the company’s AI agent Muse has been mixing up conversations across different accounts and exposing private user data, according to a new Reuters report that looked at internal company posts.

In some of these instances, employees said Muse surfaced messages about childcare requests they never made, career advice they never asked for, product orders they never placed, and bank account balances that didn’t belong to them.

One employee told colleagues that Muse had randomly sent an extra message in a chat about weather and tide information, saying: “BTW, two bank updates, your Capital One balance is $2722.58, your Charles Schwab balance is $5899.57.”

In another example, an employee using the AI agent on her personal phone received a reminder from Muse to respond to a message from her sister on Signal, the popular end-to-end encrypted messaging app.

“This seems like an extreme privacy violation. I definitely did not grant it access to other apps, or recall being asked about this,” the employee said in her post, according to the report.

Launched in September, Muse is one of the fastest-growing apps on record, with more than 10 million downloads.

Meta did not immediately respond to TechCrunch’s request for comment.

Trump, Putin and Xi release 'peace declaration' after historic meeting

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: What did Trump, Xi and Putin get out of their historic meeting?

US President Donald Trump and his Russian and Chinese counterparts Vladimir Putin and Xi Jinping have issued a "joint declaration of peace" following historic trilateral talks.

The three leaders met for 90 minutes in China's south-eastern city of Shenzhen, on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) summit.

While the presidents have previously had one-on-one meetings, these are their first trilateral talks.

In the declaration, they said "nuclear war cannot be won and must never be fought" and that the countries planned to increase the production and supply of petroleum. They also said they were "committed to reform the UN" to respond more effectively to global challenges.

Thursday's statement also flagged the issue of immunity enjoyed by state officials and said "they agreed that the International Criminal Court and other similar institutions must not abuse their jurisdiction".

Trump praised the trilateral meeting calling it "a great and highly consequential trip", in a social media post while flying back to Washington on Air Force One.

In the same post, the president announced he would host a news conference to talk about the trip on Friday at the White House.

While in China, Trump met Putin ahead of the trilateral, in their first meeting since August 2025, where the US president greeted his Russian counterpart as his "friend".

He also spoke with Xi in a separate conversation, describing the pair's relationship as "very special".

Beyond the warmth and shared niceties, however, the three men had different goals and motivations for the sit-down.

This is a remarkable diplomatic moment for China's Xi - but it comes with pitfalls

The opening remarks of the trilateral gave some indication about what was to be discussed during the closed-door meeting.

Before giving the floor to his fellow leaders, Xi said that "positive interaction among major powers is not a matter of choice, but a mandatory requirement".

Putin followed this by highlighting a shared interest in ending the Ukraine war and said the conversation would also touch on food and energy security.

China has officially remained neutral since Putin launched a full-scale invasion of Ukraine in 2022, rejecting accusations from Kyiv and its allies that it has been tacitly supporting Moscow, including by exporting dual-use technology used by the Russian military.

Beijing has also repeatedly called for negotiations and a political settlement while avoiding condemnation of Russia's aggression.

Trump greets 'my friend' Putin ahead of meeting with Xi

Meanwhile, Trump closed by saying the presidents of three of the world's largest countries have a "special responsibility" to maintain productive dialogue.

"Too often past leaders have avoided talking to nations with whom they had disagreements," he said, adding: "We feel the opposite."

Trump referenced the three countries' nuclear capabilities and said they are "uniquely positioned to discuss how to reduce the number of nuclear weapons on Earth".

It is "vital" that they co-operate to "combat the threat of nuclear proliferation in other countries around the world", he said, adding: "That's what we're doing in Iran."

"We're not going to let Iran have a nuclear weapon".

Trump then turned to Ukraine and said he and Putin have had a "long discussion" on the conflict there already.

He added that Putin "and I hope the other side, would like to see an end to that war".

Putin addressed the media following the trilateral meeting and described it as an "important chapter in the history of diplomacy".

He said Ukraine and Europe were not yet ready to make compromises to resolve the conflict in Ukraine - but he said Russia had invited EU representatives to Moscow to better understand Europe's position.

As the talks were under way in China, and even as Putin was holding his press conference claiming to be ready for peace, Ukraine's capital Kyiv was under attack from Russian drones.

Putin has repeatedly rejected all ceasefire offers from Ukraine, insisting on his maximalist demands which Kyiv and its European allies say are tantamount to de facto capitulation of Ukraine.

In Shenzhen, the Kremlin leader also vowed a "far more painful" retaliation in response to Ukrainian attacks on the bridge connecting Russia to Ukraine's occupied peninsula of Crimea. A missile and drone attack on Tuesday destroyed its central road section.

There was no mention from Putin of almost five years of Russian attacks on power plants, bridges and residential buildings in Ukraine.

The Russian president also directly addressed comparisons between his meeting with Trump and Xi and the 1945 Yalta Conference, where the leaders of the Soviet Union, Britain and the US met towards the end of World War Two.

"Nobody divided up the world at Yalta. It simply codified the realities that had emerged after WW2.

"And when it comes to today's conflicts, the realities on the ground will have to be accepted."

The three presidents spoke behind closed doors at the Kylin Villa, the exclusive resort in Shenzhen

The Kremlin released a video showing Putin leaving the summit venue and saying goodbye to Xi.

"Thank you for organising this," Putin said. "I don't think anyone even expected everything to go so well."

Meanwhile, China's president described his meeting with Putin and Trump as "positive, constructive, and highly productive", according to the country's state broadcaster CCTV.

It added that Xi recalled "the history of fighting side-by-side during WW2", as the three leaders emphasised the importance of "cherishing the hard-won peace".

Thursday's trilateral meeting was reportedly the idea of Putin.

Some Russian newspapers called it a "diplomatic victory" as after Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine many Western leaders were trying to isolate him.

The summit also marked the first time since Russia's war in Ukraine began that Putin took part in an international gathering attended by leaders from countries including Canada, Japan and Australia.

Russian 'nuclear briefcase' spotted at APEC summit

US lawmakers responded with frustration to Trump's warmth towards America's geopolitical adversaries.

"I was encouraged by some of the commitments that came out of the President's meetings in Asia, particularly the recognition that nuclear war must never be fought," Republican Senator John Curtis said in an emailed statement to the BBC.

But Curtis also said he was concerned about Trump's approach to the foreign leaders, adding that "Vladimir Putin and Xi Jinping are not our friends".

"We should not treat them as though they share our values or interests," Curtis, who sits on the Senate foreign relations committee, added.

"Doing so risks undermining our national security, weakening trust with our allies, and sending the wrong message about where America stands."

Jeanne Shaheen, a ranking Democratic member on the same committee, was more harsh in her critique, writing on X: "President Trump's 'friend' is a murderous war criminal."

With additional reporting from Brandon Drenon

Docker patches Java4Shell and the NGINX HTTP/2 with Docker Hardened Images

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

OpenJDK and Oracle disclosed Java4Shell (CVE-2026-114938) on November 17, and NGINX released fixes for two HTTP/2 flaws the next day. Docker began publishing fixed images for both in the Docker Hardened Images (DHI) catalog starting that same day.

What we know about Java4Shell is that it’s a memory-corruption bug in the Java runtime that can let an attacker with no credentials run code inside a Java application. Many container images contain a Java runtime, including application images that bundle their own, so it affects teams well beyond the ones that write Java.

Docker Hardened Images are the trusted foundation your applications should run on, because a flaw in the runtime inside an image is fixed only when that image is rebuilt, and Docker rebuilds its hardened images as soon as upstream fixes become available. Fixed images for the Amazon Corretto, SapMachine and Azul Zulu runtimes and for NGINX are in the catalog today, with Eclipse Temurin following as soon as Adoptium completes its fixed builds.

To decode Base64 quickly, the HotSpot JVM uses a hand-written routine built on AVX-512 instructions. That routine miscalculates a length when the input ends in unexpected padding and writes up to 62 bytes past the end of a buffer on the Java heap. Calif, the researchers who found it, turned that into heap disclosure and remote code execution.

They demonstrated code execution before authentication against default installations of Keycloak, Jira, Confluence, Artifactory, Jenkins and PingFederate, and after authentication against Kafka, Solr, Elasticsearch, Gerrit and Spinnaker. They note there is no universal exploit: each application needs its own, and they have not published those. As of November 18 there are no public reports of exploitation in the wild.

Oracle rates the issue 8.1 (High) under CVSS 3.1. The researchers rate it 9.2 (Critical) under CVSS 4.0. CISA has issued an alert urging immediate patching.

To check a host’s CPU, run this on the host or Kubernetes node. Hardened runtime images have no shell, so it will not run inside the container.

grep -m1 ‘^flags’ /proc/cpuinfo | tr ‘ ‘ ‘\n’ | grep -E ‘^(avx512f|avx512bw|avx512vbmi|bmi2)$’

The host is affected if all four flags are printed.

Two other ways to check, for when a shell on the node is hard to get.

Ask the JVM. This works in hardened images:

docker run –rm –entrypoint java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version | grep -w UseAVX

UseAVX = 3 means the JVM is using AVX-512 on that host. Treat the host as affected until the full check says otherwise. A value of 0 to 2 means AVX-512 is not in use. On ARM64 nothing is printed. The answer applies only to the host the command ran on.

Ask Kubernetes. kubectl get nodes -L kubernetes.io/arch,node.kubernetes.io/instance-type shows the architecture and instance type of every node. If you run Node Feature Discovery, nodes with the instruction set carry the label feature.node.kubernetes.io/cpu-cpuid.AVX512VBMI=true.

Check build runners and CI workers as well. They run Java, and they talk to the network.

Ask the JVM. This works in hardened images:

docker run –rm –entrypoint java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version | grep -w UseAVX

UseAVX = 3 means the JVM is using AVX-512 on that host. Treat the host as affected until the full check says otherwise. A value of 0 to 2 means AVX-512 is not in use. On ARM64 nothing is printed. The answer applies only to the host the command ran on.

Ask Kubernetes. kubectl get nodes -L kubernetes.io/arch,node.kubernetes.io/instance-type shows the architecture and instance type of every node. If you run Node Feature Discovery, nodes with the instruction set carry the label feature.node.kubernetes.io/cpu-cpuid.AVX512VBMI=true.

Check build runners and CI workers as well. They run Java, and they talk to the network.

Fixed. The published images report 17.0.21.1.2, 21.0.13.1.2, 25.0.5.1.2 and 27.0.1.1.4.

Fixed. The published images report 21.0.13.1+2, 25.0.5.1+2 and 27.0.1.1+4.

Fixed. The published Zulu images we checked report 17.0.21.1+2, 21.0.13.1+2, 25.0.5.1+2 and 27.0.1.1+4.

Waiting for Adoptium to complete its release. On November 18 Adoptium had published 27.0.1.1+4 for x86-64 Linux only. 17.0.21.1, 21.0.13.1 and 25.0.5.1 were still in testing. Adoptium tracks progress in adoptium/temurin#326. Our published images still report 17.0.21+9, 21.0.13+9, 25.0.5+8 and 27.0.1.0.2+1, which are vulnerable.

Java 26 reached end of support in September 2026, so no fix is expected from any vendor, and it is not among the fixed lines in the OpenJDK advisory. If you run it, treat it as affected and keep the flag set. We recommend moving to Java 25 or updating to Java 27. If you cannot migrate yet, ask us whether Extended Lifecycle Support can cover your version. It is the Docker Hardened Images Enterprise add-on for software past its upstream end of life.

Not affected by this CVE and no action is needed for it. The same November update fixes other issues in these lines, so update when new images arrive.

Application images that bundle Java, such as Keycloak, Jenkins, Kafka, Elasticsearch and Solr

They follow the bundled runtime, which for many of them is Temurin, and they are rebuilt once the runtime they use is fixed. Until then, set the flag yourself.

Set the mitigation flag yourself on every JVM that is still waiting for a fixed runtime, and check what each image already sets before you do. Treat the flag as a stopgap, because a JVM is protected only if the flag reaches it when it starts, and a deployment setting or a start script can override it or drop it. A fixed runtime is the only thing that closes the issue, so move to the fixed image as soon as it is published.

The CPU check and the mitigation flag in the steps below apply to any image that runs a HotSpot-based OpenJDK build. The version strings in this post come from Docker Hardened Images, so for an image from another source, confirm the fixed build in that source’s advisory.

The fixed build can only come from whoever builds your image, because the Java runtime and the system packages your application runs on come from the image underneath it. That dependency is why a hardened image is a necessary step toward a trusted software supply chain. With Docker Hardened Images, tracking each vendor’s fix and rebuilding on it is work Docker does for you. The images are minimal and secure by default, and they are built to serve as a trusted and verifiable upstream for your builds. You can move a service onto one by following the migration guide.

A container often runs more than one JVM, for example when a build tool forks a JVM for tests, an application server starts helper processes or a CI agent starts a JVM for every build step. The flag protects only the JVMs that receive it, and the variable most people reach for first is JAVA_OPTS, which Java does not read at all.

The JVM itself, however it is started. Includes javac, keytool and other JDK tools.

A Java 8 JVM that sees it refuses to start. Some DHI FIPS images already set this variable.

Not read by javac, keytool or other JDK tools. DHI FIPS images set it. Java 8 ignores it.

JAVA_OPTS, JENKINS_JAVA_OPTS, MAVEN_OPTS, ES_JAVA_OPTS and similar

A start script, which copies the value onto one java command line

Each script has its own rules. If nothing in the image reads the variable, it does nothing.

Five rules follow from that table. We tested each on DHI images.

JAVA_OPTS only works where a script reads it. With JAVA_OPTS=”-XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics” set and a plain java command, the intrinsic stays on. No warning is printed.

A flag on the command line stops at that process. We started a JVM with the flag on its command line and had it start a second JVM. The child ran with the intrinsic on. With JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS or JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS in the environment, the child ran with it off. A child started with a cleared environment gets nothing, whichever variable you use.

Check what the image already sets before you set anything. In Docker and Kubernetes, your value replaces the image’s value. It is not appended.

docker inspect –format ‘{{range .Config.Env}}{{println .}}{{end}}’ \  | grep -E ‘^(JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS|JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS)=’

Our FIPS images use these variables to configure the FIPS provider. dhi.io/eclipse-temurin:21-fips sets both. When we replaced its JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS with only the flag, the JVM started normally, but the org.bouncycastle.fips.approved_only property was gone. Nothing warned us. If the command prints a value, copy it and add the flag at the end:

env:  – name: JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS    value: “ -XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics”

Not every FIPS image uses the same variables, so run the check for each image.

Keep JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS away from Java 8. Java 8 is not affected and does not know the flag:

Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS: -XX:-UseBASE64IntrinsicsUnrecognized VM option ‘UseBASE64Intrinsics’Error: Could not create the Java Virtual Machine.

Adding -XX:+IgnoreUnrecognizedVMOptions to the variable does not help on Java 8. Where Java 8 shares an environment with a newer Java, either scope the variable to the containers that need it or use JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, which Java 8 ignores. Java 11 accepts the flag, so it is harmless there.

Verify the process, not the environment. Running java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version inside a container starts a new JVM. It tells you what the environment gives a new JVM, not what the JVM already serving traffic was started with. For the running process, use the startup log line. JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS prints one too, starting with NOTE: Picked up. In an image that includes the JDK you can also ask the process directly:

jcmd VM.flags | tr ‘ ‘ ‘\n’ | grep UseBASE64Intrinsics

The answer must be -XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics. If the flag reached the JVM through an environment variable, it will not show in the process’s command line, so ps is not a reliable check.

Some applications discard these variables on purpose. Elasticsearch’s start script, for example, ignores JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS and expects ES_JAVA_OPTS. Check your application’s documentation.

Pull the new image and redeploy. Four things catch people out:

Then confirm with java -version that what is running reports a fixed build. After that, remove the flag from every place you set it.

The day after Java4Shell, NGINX released fixes for two memory errors in the way it handles HTTP/2 connections, tracked as CVE-2026-123007 and CVE-2026-123633. A crafted request can crash a worker process, and where an attacker gets past the operating system’s memory protections it can run code inside that worker. Both flaws need HTTP/2 to be enabled on the server, and the same releases fix other NGINX issues that do not depend on HTTP/2, so update either way.

The Docker Hardened Images for NGINX were rebuilt on NGINX 1.30.7 and 1.31.9 and published on November 18, the same day as the upstream release. To patch, pull the image again and redeploy, and move any tag pinned to an older exact version to a fixed one. nginx -v prints the version, and the fixed ones are 1.30.7 and 1.31.9. The notes on pull policy, mirrors and your own images in the section above apply to NGINX images in the same way.

The vulnerable code is in the Java runtime inside the image, and if you are using DHI, shipping the fix becomes our job. Whether that code can be reached in your deployment depends on things an image cannot see: the CPU it is scheduled onto, the options you start it with, the processes it starts, and the traffic you send it.

Track every Java vendor and ship fixed runtimes in DHI as soon as they are released

Track every Java vendor yourself and rebuild your base images on each fixed runtime

Tell you which images are fixed and which are still waiting

Find out from each vendor which of its builds are fixed

Tell you what we know, and update this post

Follow the OpenJDK, Oracle and vendor advisories as they change

Three jobs stay with you whichever image you run.

It was only four weeks ago that the news was PHP2Shell and the eMudhra certificate compromise. Calif, the security research team that found Java4Shell and helped find PHP2Shell, says it used AI to find this flaw in about an hour and that the whole effort cost less than $200 in tokens. When a flaw like this can be found and weaponized at that price, it is reasonable to expect more of them.

If you lead a team or answer for how your organization builds and ships software, the questions after a week like this one are whether you were exposed and how quickly you can prove you were not. It is imperative to have a trusted foundation in place before the next flaw appears, and Docker Hardened Images is that foundation to adopt. This week Docker began publishing fixed Java and NGINX images to the catalog within a day of each disclosure, with the images still waiting listed in the status table above. Each image ships with a signed SBOM and SLSA Build Level 3 provenance, so you have a record of what it contains and how it was built.

The Docker Hardened Images catalog is free and open source under Apache 2.0, and moving one of your services onto a hardened image typically means changing one line in its Dockerfile. If you are not building on Docker Hardened Images yet, start today.

Zesty's Autonomous Kubernetes Platform Now Spans Compute and GPU Infrastructure Ahead of AWS re:Invent 2026

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Following the launch of Automated Fractional GPUs, CTO and Co-Founder Alexey Baikov will speak at re:Invent on keeping Kubernetes infrastructure cost-efficient as AI adoption scales

SAN MATEO, Calif., Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Zesty, a leader in Kubernetes optimization, today announced that its autonomous optimization platform now spans both Kubernetes compute and GPU infrastructure ahead of AWS re:Invent 2026 in Las Vegas. With the recent addition of Automated Fractional GPUs, Zesty now manages GPU memory, and workload placement alongside the CPU, memory, storage, and cloud commitment decisions it already handles, adjusting each one as demand changes.

New workloads such as GPU-based inference and AI agents bring expensive resources and unpredictable demand. At the same time, AI is speeding up how engineering works. Developers ship faster, and platform teams  are expected to keep pace with tighter SLOs and infrastructure cost targets but no extra headcount or budget.

These shifts put new strain on how teams size Kubernetes resources. Today, 66% of organizations hosting generative AI models use Kubernetes to manage some or all of their inference workloads, and GPUs for those workloads are costly and hard to come by.

GPU resource needs vary across workloads and shift over time, making memory allocation and placement difficult to manage. Allocate too little, and workloads risk out-of-memory (OOM) failures or degraded performance. Allocate too much, and expensive capacity sits idle.

With GPU availability already limited, these mismatches leave less capacity for new workloads, forcing teams to delay projects or pay for additional GPUs they may struggle to secure.

Zesty's Automated Fractional GPUs decides on an ongoing basis which workloads should share each GPU and how much GPU memory each one gets. It then revises those decisions as demand rises and falls, staying within the performance and availability policies each team sets. Supporting NVIDIA's CUDA-native GPU fractioning, the agent installs in the cluster with no application code or model changes and no restart of running workloads."GPU availability is a real constraint, yet much of the capacity companies already have goes unused. Teams end up buying more GPUs at a high cost before fully utilizing the ones they own." said Alexey Baikov, CTO and Co-Founder of Zesty. " Zesty automatically manages GPU sharing and resource allocation so teams can run more workloads on existing GPUs while protecting performance."

Alexey Baikov will expand on these themes at re:Invent in a lightning talk, "Optimizing Kubernetes in the Age of AI" (CON205-S) on Wednesday, December 2 at 1 p.m. local time in the Global Impact Zone at the Venetian. The session covers how AI-generated applications increase resource waste and offers practical strategies for reducing it, from rightsizing resource requests to sharing GPUs. It also examines why native autoscaling tools alone are not enough and how continuous optimization across pods, nodes, GPUs, and cloud commitments can help teams control costs while protecting performance. Finally, it looks at how platform teams can maintain cost accountability without slowing developer velocity.

Attendees can also meet the Zesty team at booth #1658 throughout the week.

About ZestyZesty helps organizations reduce infrastructure costs through autonomous compute and GPU resource optimization. The platform continuously matches resources to real-time workload demand, automatically eliminating overprovisioning and improving infrastructure efficiency while protecting application performance. With Automated Fractional GPUs, Zesty is extending this same approach to GPU infrastructure, helping teams get more from existing GPU capacity while controlling the growing cost of AI workloads. By replacing constant manual rightsizing and resource tuning with policy-driven automation, Zesty enables teams to run leaner Kubernetes environments without adding operational risk.

Founded in 2019, Zesty is focused on making infrastructure more efficient, automated, and cost-effective.

7 Surfaces You Should Never Use Ice Melt On

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Avoid damaging salt-sensitive outdoor surfaces with these expert-backed suggestions.

Ice melt salt is an effective option for preventing and removing ice from sidewalks, driveways and stairs, but it isn’t suitable for every surface. Applying ice melt to certain materials can cause significant damage and should be avoided. Ahead, learn which surfaces are most vulnerable to damage from ice melt, why it happens and what safer alternatives you can use to keep outdoor areas free from ice.

Ice melt salt chemically reacts with certain materials, accelerates freeze-thaw cycles and alters surface moisture levels to cause damage. The type of damage will depend on the surface and how it interacts with ice melt.

Ice melt contains rock salt, calcium chloride and/or magnesium chloride to melt ice faster and at lower temperatures. While all melting salt is corrosive and can damage outdoor surfaces, ice melt products can react differently than rock salt alone.

Ice melt seeps deeply into porous cement-based materials. The calcium chloride in ice melt can react with elements of concrete or brick to form calcium oxychloride crystals. As the crystals grow, they generate extreme pressure that can cause cracks, surface spalling and crumbling.

De-icing products accelerate freeze-thaw cycles in porous cement-based surfaces, causing them to break down. “It makes the freezing and thawing cycle more aggressive, which is what causes the surface to break up,” Murphy says.

Though less damaging than rock salt, ice melt can still contribute to deterioration. Calcium chloride and magnesium chloride attract and retain moisture, increasing the potential for freeze-thaw damage.

Conversely, ice melt draws moisture out of organic materials, causing them to dry out. Over time, this moisture loss can lead to warping, cracking, splitting and other forms of deterioration.

Ice melt accelerates metal oxidation to create rust. It can corrode rebar embedded in concrete, potentially causing cracks and structural damage, as well as rusting exposed metal stairs and railings.

It’s best to avoid applying salt to these salt-sensitive surfaces.

“New concrete is the most vulnerable because it takes weeks to fully cure, so salt on the surface will cause the top few millimeters to flake off,” Murphy says. To be safe, avoid salting new concrete for the first season.

“Cracked concrete opens up for water to get inside, and salt in the water will only make that worse because it will freeze and thaw more aggressively,” Murphy says.

All ice melt products will damage hard-scape surfaces due to chemical reactions and freeze-thaw cycles. “If you are using them, use them sparingly and remove them as soon as possible to decrease continuous exposure for your hardscape areas,” Zurcher says.

Natural stone like limestone and flagstone can react to ice melt compounds. The porous surface is vulnerable to damaging freeze-thaw cycles as well.

Harsh de-icers can damage dyed, painted or stamped concrete surfaces by breaking down color pigments.

Metal stairs and railings will rapidly rust when salt is applied to them, so it’s best to avoid it.

Ice melt draws moisture from wood, causing cracking, warping and raised fibers. It also degrades paint and stain and leaves behind chalky white salt stains.

Sand: Sand doesn’t melt ice, but does provide traction to prevent slips.

Wood ash: Instead of throwing away wood ash, use it as a salt alternative to melt ice and also provide improved traction.

Electric mats: Installed before cold weather sets in, electric melting mats warm up and prevent ice from ever forming. These can be very effective on stairs and sidewalks.

Weed torch: Weed torches are handy when you need to melt a lot of ice quickly. Do not use one to melt ice on wood and avoid painted or stained concrete as well.

“The best way to reduce damage from these chemicals is to shovel snow as soon as possible,” Zurcher says, so you can use less of them. Use ice melt sparingly, and only as directed. A handful is enough to treat three square yards. Instead of using ice melt alone, mix a small amount with sand to treat surfaces.

Regular sealing protects cement-based products.  “Reseal every three to five years to make sure no water can get in, salty or not,” Murphy says. Additionally, use chloride-free de-icing products, or those labelled as “concrete-safe”.

Newly poured concrete is the most vulnerable to salt damage, but all concrete surfaces will experience damage with enough prolonged exposure to salt.

It depends on the extent of the damage. Major cracks and breakage mean concrete needs to be removed and repoured, Zurcher says, while surface damage could be skim-coated, with potentially limited results.

Pet-safe products will have similar effects on hardscape areas, Zurcher says, as they still contain salt.

Google staff push back on cutting off Search for schools that reject AI

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Schools that switch off Google's AI tools will lose Search too, and staff warn that could drive them away.

More than 540 Google employees and contractors have signed a union petition asking the company to let K-12 schools use Search without its AI features, Bloomberg reported. Google plans to turn AI off by default for students, but schools that want Search must accept the AI that comes with it.

More than 540 Google employees and contractors have signed a petition asking the company to let schools use Google Search without its AI features, Bloomberg reported on Thursday. The Alphabet Workers Union, which rallied staff over job security in July, organised the letter.

“Cutting off access to Search for schools that don’t wish to use AI will not help persuade our users,” the workers wrote. They warned that schools could end up “shutting out Google products altogether.”

The letter is addressed to CEO Sundar Pichai, head of Search Elizabeth Reid and education chief Chris Phillips. Google did not immediately comment to Bloomberg.

Earlier this month, Google said it would switch off Gemini and Search for K-12 student accounts unless schools turn them on. But AI is built into Search, so schools that switch Search back on get its AI features too. Schools that want no AI at all must go without Google Search.

The plan drew criticism from parents and schools. Senator Ed Markey wrote to Google on 9 November, asking whether schools could keep Search with the AI switched off. In July, Common Sense Media called Google’s AI Search an “unacceptable risk” for students.

The stakes are high for Google. Its Chrome operating system, which will stop getting updates in 2034, ran on 58% of laptops and tablets shipped to US schools last year, per Futuresource Consulting.

Earlier this month, a family sued Google over its daughter’s conversations with its AI tools on a school device, and Google said it was reviewing the claims. AI companies already face a growing wave of lawsuits over alleged harms to users, including children.

Leaving Google is not easy for schools, said Dan Carroll, co-founder of edtech startup Clever. Chromebooks are built around Google’s software, and switching search engines takes time and training.

“It’s a real strategic pickle,” Carroll said.

Russia Opens Legal Bitcoin, Ethereum and USDT Trading to Retail Investors Under New Rules

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Russia finally allowed retail access to Bitcoin, Ethereum, and USDT on regulated exchange operators under its new rules that took effect today, Nov. 17.

Russia has finally opened its crypto market for retail customers as its central bank today enabled trading in Bitcoin, Ethereum, and USDT for the general public. With the law signed by President Vladimir Putin, the Bank of Russia officially rolled out BTC, ETH, and USDT for public trading on domestic exchanges, beginning Nov. 17, 2026.

The new regulation, announced by the Bank of Russia last week, took effect today, Nov. 17. The move allows individual customers to purchase these assets through intermediaries, including brokers, exchanges, and authorized crypto marketplaces. This means ordinary investors can now buy up to 300,000 rubles ($3,700) in a year through a single broker, exchange, or asset manager, after passing a mandatory test.

The announcement expands access to regulated crypto trading in the country and connects the assets with TradFi. This allows non-qualified investors to buy and trade BTC, ETH, and USDT directly through traditional exchanges and clearing systems.

However, qualified investors are not subject to such asset limits and trading restrictions.

The development points out that Russia has taken decisive action to deepen the integration of the crypto market with traditional finance. The debut indicates surging retail interest in cryptocurrency amid regulatory evolution.

It signals a strategic shift in how traditional exchanges handle digital assets. Accommodating retail clients signals growing confidence in the crypto market’s maturity. This highlights rising demand for coordinated exposure to crypto assets within Russia’s capital markets.

The announcement introduces new entry gateways that give retail investors organized access to the crypto market, redefining how these assets are traded locally and internationally.

In the past, crypto in Russia operated in a highly restrictive, complex regulatory environment.  Now, the regulator is moving toward a balance between investor freedom and financial stability. In July, the central bank opened the door to crypto for companies to facilitate global trade. This month, it introduced new legislation that allows retail investors to trade crypto within specified limits.

Africa's Digital Progress Accelerates, but 64% Usage Gap Shows Next Challenge for Policymakers

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GSMA Digital Africa Index 2026 finds seven more countries joining the continent's leading group for digital development and enabling policy

CAPE TOWN, South Africa, Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Africa is making measurable progress on digital development and enabling policy, but turning widespread mobile broadband coverage into meaningful digital participation remains a critical challenge, according to the GSMA's flagship Digital Africa Index 2026, launched today at the GSMA Digital Africa Summit Cape Town.

Mobile broadband networks now cover 92% of Africa's population, yet only 28% use mobile internet on their own devices, leaving a 64% usage gap. The cost of internet-enabled devices limited digital skills, and a lack of relevant local content and services remain significant barriers. While the usage gap is a global challenge, it is largest in Africa, making accelerating adoption particularly urgent.

The Index, drawing on data from 54 countries, also points to encouraging policy momentum. Ten African countries now combine stronger digital development with more enabling policy and regulation, up from three in 2024. Egypt, Ghana, Nigeria, Rwanda, Senegal, Seychelles and Tunisia have joined Kenya, Mauritius and South Africa in the Index's leading group.

Angela Wamola, Head of Africa, GSMA, said: "Africa has made real progress in expanding mobile broadband coverage. The challenge now is ensuring that more people can actually use it. With 64% of the population living within coverage but not using mobile internet, closing the usage gap requires us to tackle the barriers that continue to keep people offline.

"South Africa shows what targeted reform can achieve. Making smartphones more affordable, building digital skills and ensuring people have access to relevant, trusted digital services can help turn connectivity into meaningful economic and social participation."

Device affordability remains a key barrier. South Africa demonstrates how targeted policy reform can support adoption: after removing the 9% excise duty on smartphones priced below R2,500 in April 2025, monthly sales of entry-level smartphones increased by 80%, from around 250,000 in March 2025 to around 460,000 in February 2026. The GSMA is urging governments where duties or sales taxes still apply to entry-level smartphones to remove those charges.

The Index calls for coordinated action across five areas: affordable and reliable connectivity; public value from digital services; trust and accountability; inclusive digital service design; and secure, connected government systems.

I Think I Should Have Seen That Bond Casting Coming When Slow Horses Dropped Its Renewal Announcement

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fans Have BIG Reactions To Robert Pattinson Becoming The New Bond (And Pass The Popcorn)

Now That We Know Who Is Playing James Bond, Who Could Be The Next Bond Girl?

The Full Circle Moment Robert Pattinson (Kinda) Told Us Zendaya Is Responsible For His 007 Role

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Last Monday morning was an exciting time for fans of British spy thrillers. First, Slow Horses was renewed for Seasons 9 and 10, meaning we’ll be getting at least four more seasons of the hit show, available with an Apple TV subscription. Then, just hours later, Amazon MGM finally dropped the bomb we've all been waiting for and announced that the next actor to play James Bond would be Robert Pattinson. Many of us had been waiting for that for months. Years, even, yet it still came as a surprise, given the latest rumors. In hindsight, I probably should have been clued in that the announcement was imminent after the Slow Horses news dropped.

Over the last few weeks and months, there have been a ton of rumors regarding the next Bond ahead of the long-awaited Bond 26 finally beginning production (we’re still not sure when that will actually happen). Names like Jacob Elordi, Henry Cavill, Callum Turner, and more have been rumored. Slow Horses star Jack Lowden was also heavily rumored in recent weeks. Lowden, who plays River Cartwright on the show about disgraced MI5 agents, was looking to make the jump to MI6 and become the ninth man to play Bond.

It’s certainly possible for an actor to appear in multiple projects, but given Slow Horses' aggressive filming schedule (they have already wrapped Season 7, due next year, and are filming Season 8), it might have been a tough schedule to work out for Lowden had he been named Bond. When the next two seasons were announced, it should have clued me in that Lowden likely wasn’t going to be James Bond going forward. It’s quite possible that Apple may have actually been waiting on the Bond decision to announce it was moving forward with more seasons of Slow Horses, too. So, when the news dropped about the show's renewal, I should have known Bond’s announcement was on deck.

Interestingly, despite the announcements coming almost at the same time, there are also rumors that Lowden might not be part of Seasons 9 and 10. A report in Variety calls Lowden’s return “uncertain.” I’m not sure what to make of that. The book-to-screen adaptation has been adapting one book per season, generally. Season 6 was an exception, as it combined two books, the 6th and 7th in Mick Herron’s series Slough House. That's not all, though.

Warning! There are some spoilers ahead from the books Slow Horses is adapted from!

That would presumably mean that Season 7 will be an adaptation of book #8, Bad Actors. As fans of the TV show know, Season 6 ended on a cliffhanger with River having been poisoned by the Russian assassins. Book readers know that River spends Bad Actors on the sidelines, recovering from the assassination attempt, and doesn’t appear in the novel at all. If the show has adapted Bad Actors for Season 7, might Lowden be mostly absent? Cartwright does return in the ninth book, Clown Town, which we do know is going to be Season 8 of the show.

Here’s where it gets interesting. As of today, Clown Town is the most recent book to be published in the Slough House series, so we don’t know what Herron or the producers intend to take the series, so all eyes are on Lowden!

How to Recover From High School

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 19, 2026 | Reviewed by Michelle Quirk

High school may have been a high for many. But if it was a low for you, you ain't alone. A 2025 YouGov survey found that 12 percent of respondents disliked high school while 8 percent hated it. Consider me among the 8 percent.

Having a lot more "grrrrs" than "rah-rahs" in high school could leave you holding onto Rs—namely, resentment and regret—which could keep you trapped in your high school days—and daze—way longer than you'd want. Here's how to instead truly graduate from all that:

A friend once described high school as "The last time that you had no choice of who is going to be around you most of the time," because your high school totally depended on where your family happened to live and what resources and connections they happened to have. Therefore, chances were quite high that you were around high schoolers who didn't fit very well with you.

My high school, for example, had very little diversity and was filled with people not used to dealing with those of different racial, ethnic, cultural, and socioeconomic backgrounds and ways of thinking. For example, my high school soccer team had never seen someone like me play soccer. So, it was pretty common to hear racial stereotypes and epithets hurled around throughout my teenage years, making me feel quite alone then.

This brings up an age-old problem—or perhaps an age-young one. Your high school classmates were in their mid-teens at the time. Their brains and senses of self were still developing like fruitcake batter that had just entered the oven. That, along with their lack of life experience, left them quite limited in their ability to process things around them, know right from wrong, and think for themselves. There are reasons why rental car companies still charge extra until you've gotten past your 25th birthday.

Does that guarantee that all of your high school classmates will eventually grow out of their high school selves? Of course not. Nevertheless, surely you wouldn't base your worth on what any teenager would say about you now? Heck, I wouldn't even listen to my own high school self.

That's because I was still full of "Never Have I Evers" back then—way before I had actually traveled around the world, met a much greater diversity of people, developed real friendships, and engaged in real romantic relationships. For a while, it was hard to look back at my naive high school self without a ton of cringe. But I have since learned to forgive my teenage self—recognizing that I didn't know better at the time.

Part of healing is forgiving your high school classmates as well. For example, the female classmate who once told me, "You are smart and funny, so I like hanging out with you, but you are so incredibly ugly," probably wasn't used to seeing a diverse range of looks and people. Also, her frontal lobe may not yet have been developed enough to tell her, "OK, maybe don't say something like that if you want that person to help you with your homework or teach you how to play sports."

Of course, the fact that to save money my dad—rather than, you know, a real barber—would regularly cut my hair—leaving my head looking like that of a LEGO person—probably didn't help. But eventually you realize that you can recover from not only a bad haircut but also high school. After all, high school is only four years and not forever—lasting about as long as a pair of heavily distressed jeans.

It is important, though, to treat any distressing high school experience like you would a pair of out-of-fashion distressed jeans. Throw them away so that they don't keep taking up space—space that you could otherwise use for new memories, experiences, people, and growth. In fact, get rid of anything that may trigger low high school memories, such as severing ties with anyone who doesn't recognize that you have outgrown your high school years. If you do find it difficult to move on from those years, get help from others, such as a professional therapist.

You'll need to free up that mental and emotional space because you may have a lot of catch-up growing to do. I certainly did, which probably delayed a lot of things and led to a lot of late blooming. But don't despair. Think of going through life as what the character Kandi said on an episode of "Two and a Half Men" about being with Alan: "[It's like being] in the back seat of a car driven by a really smart kangaroo. He may go up on the curb a couple of times, but he'll get you there." The key then is to learn from your faulty high school days—understand what went wrong, what you did wrong, and learn from it. Adapt accordingly.

Now, throwing stuff out doesn't mean simply suppressing your bad high school memories and feelings. That can be like hiding a ferret in your pants. It could end up eating at your rather sensitive spots over time and emerging at the most unexpected, inopportune times. Instead, consider directly confronting your past and processing it.

In the process, you may even find that your memories don't match reality. The supposedly cool things you missed out on may not have been so cool. Those who made you feel bad may have actually felt very bad about themselves.

In lieu of going to my high school reunion, I had dinner with someone I actually liked from high school. We had never really talked about our high school experiences until then. But it soon became apparent that her high school experience was similarly negative. When you are open about such experiences, you may end up realizing that you weren't quite as alone as you thought you were.

Bruce Y. Lee, M.D., M.B.A., is a writer, journalist, professor, systems modeler, computational and digital health expert, physician, avocado-eater, and entrepreneur, not always in that order.

Why Rockstar Games Includes Bigfoot And UFOs To Find In Its Big Open-World Games: ‘We Love That Stuff’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BigfootGrand Theft Auto 6GTA 6Rob NelsonRockstar Games

For nearly as long as Rockstar has been making big open-world games, like the recently released Grand Theft Auto 6 and 2018’s Red Dead Redemption 2, players have been exploring these worlds for strange secrets, supernatural events, and cryptids like Bigfoot and Mothman. And sometimes they actually find things along these lines, which made me curious as to why Rockstar includes these oddities.

I spoke to Rob Nelson, Rockstar North’s head of production and the de facto boss on GTA 6, ahead of the massive game’s launch. And during our interview, I asked him why so many of its open-world games contain otherworldly or hard-to-explain mysteries and creatures. According to him, there are a few reasons why Rockstar added things like the Skunk Ape, which players have already found lurking in the swamps of Leonida, to GTA 6. For one thing, they have the “team size” to be able to work on all of this extra content in a way they couldn’t long ago. The other reason: “We love it.”

“Also, the fact that Florida is so fucking weird and crazy, we love that stuff,” said Nelson. “We love sort of playing with players. You never know whether it’s real paranormal stuff or it’s phony paranormal stuff. Is it really Bigfoot? Is it a guy wearing a Bigfoot mask? We just love it.”

Nelson explained that the team also wanted the map in GTA 6 to feel completely full and packed with things to do and see. They wanted it to be “constantly surprising you.” It also helps that, according to Nelson, the team working on GTA 6 has been together for “such a long time” and that there are entire groups within the studio made up of people who in the past were responsible for a lot more. Now, they can focus on smaller details and specific bits of each game, like adding ghosts in GTA V or a vampire in Red Dead Redemption 2.Â

“They’re dedicated to making these games amazing things,” said Nelson in a way that really made it seem like he was proud of his team.Â

Another reason GTA 6 has so much weird and strange stuff in it is because of how much wilderness is in the game. And Nelson and his team wanted to fill these parts of the map with just as much to do and see as the urban areas. ”

“[We] always want it to feel like there’s something weird out there,” said Nelson. “And hopefully in GTA 6 there will be stuff for people to be finding for a long time to come.â

Arthur Hayes predicts 97% crypto rally weeks after he exited trades

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

His targets also include $2,800 for Ethereum. Hayes gave no deadline and called the setup low conviction.

Arthur Hayes has just set bullish price targets for Ethereum, Zcash, NEAR and Venice Token, less than a month after saying he closed Maelstrom’s directional trading book. His $1,800 Zcash target was 97.3% above the token’s Nov. 18.

In a Nov. 17 post, Hayes called the tokens his “Four Horsemen.” He put ETH at $2,800, ZEC at $1,800, NEAR at $6 and VVV at $40. He described the prospective trade size as “small to medium,” expressed little conviction and expected a rally before the end of what he called the AI bubble.

The post gave no deadline for the targets. Hayes gave no details about purchases by him or Maelstrom, or about the fund’s current exposure.

On Oct. 24, CryptoSlate reported that Hayes said he had closed Maelstrom’s directional book and taken profits on ETH, Ethena, Uniswap and other tokens. The exit covered bets on which way prices would move. ETH was among the tokens he sold and one of the four he expects to rally.

Hayes set out his condition for staying on the sidelines in a Nov. 5 essay. He wrote that Maelstrom would wait unless the fiat liquidity picture changed dramatically. His concern was that a decline in AI stocks could make lenders demand higher yields from those companies. That would make debt harder to issue and, in his view, leave less dollar liquidity available to markets.

NEAR and ZEC were names Hayes had publicly walked away from before. In a June 8 essay, he wrote that he had sold NEAR the previous week and gave a separate reason for exiting ZEC, citing a bug in Zcash’s Orchard privacy pool. The two tokens are now back in his public outlook, and ZEC has the widest gap to its target.
